140:- [x] ✅ Batch resolution — resolve batching regex to matched files, pick latest or specific batch — `resolve_batch` (`datasources/flatfile.py`): a batch `pattern` is a regex whose **first capture group is the batch key**; `latest` selects the greatest key (lexicographic — ISO dates sort chronologically), falling back to the storage modified-time when the pattern has no group; `specific` selects the file whose key equals a given `batch`. `BatchNotFoundError` on no match / unknown batch. The filter+select logic is pure and fully unit-tested (8 tests); object listing (`list_files` — S3 `list_objects_v2` paginated / ADLS `list_blobs`) is the live seam, and `resolve_batch_file` composes list+resolve. _Wiring a check's target to a resolved batch path is the Week-5 `run_suite` dispatch._
143:- [x] ✅ UC table check path — UC table → GX DataFrame datasource → run suite — `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` (`datasources/unity_catalog.py`): reads the target table from the Databricks SQL Warehouse into a pandas DataFrame (`pd.read_sql_table` via the databricks SQLAlchemy dialect — reflection quotes identifiers, so no hand-built SQL) and runs the suite's GX expectations against GX's **pandas DataFrame asset** — the "GX DataFrame datasource" shape (§5), the same shape Databricks Labs DQX consumes, so v1.1 swaps GX→DQX behind this one interface. Reuses the shared `gx_runner` (`run_expectations` + result mapping) exactly like the flat-file runner; the `catalog` is pinned in the connection URL (`build_databricks_url`, now shared with the column profiler — no duplication) so the 2-level `schema.table` resolves to `catalog.schema.table`. Because GX runs **in-process on the DataFrame**, the run path is unit-tested with a canned frame (real GX execution); only the reflect+read (`_read_table`) is the deferred-smoke seam. `build_unity_catalog_runner` mirrors `build_snowflake_runner` (raw config + resolved PAT, not the ORM). 14 tests; `unity_catalog` 91% (only `_read_table` uncovered). _Connection-type → runner dispatch is the Week-5 `run_suite` wiring._

205	- [x] ✅ **Manual run trigger + run/result read API** — `POST /suites/{id}/run` queues a `Run` and dispatches it via the shared `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` helper (**edit**-gated — the capability ladder grants "trigger runs" at edit); the suite target (#215) is resolved **up front** so a targetless/misconfigured suite fails fast with 422 instead of a queued→failed run, and a broker outage marks the run `failed` + returns 503 (same contract as the probe). New `api/v1/runs.py` read surface for the results page: `GET /runs?suite_id=&status=&limit=` and `GET /runs/{id}` (run + joined `Result`s) are **suite-scoped** — the list filters to accessible suites and the detail gates on `require_permission(view)`, so per-suite sharing + existence-hiding hold (exactly why direct-Postgres/Grafana is rejected as the primary surface); `GET /pipeline_runs?provider=&status=` is the orchestration monitoring feed (auth-only, not suite-scoped). Read helpers live in `run_service.list_runs/get_run/list_results` + `orchestration_service.list_pipeline_runs`. 14 DB-backed API tests (trigger happy/targetless-422/edit-403/no-access-404/broker-503; list scoping+filters+limit; detail+results / 404s; pipeline filters + auth). _(Run-enablement gap; PR-C0b; gates the Week-6 Results page PR-C1)_
206	- [x] ✅ Run progress API — poll endpoint returning per-check live status — `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (suite-scoped): run lifecycle status + per-check resolution (status or null=pending) + a status histogram, the compact shape the live-progress UI polls. DB-driven (worker writes status + result rows); GX's atomic batch means per-check rows land at completion, so this reports lifecycle + final resolution (documented). **[PR #301](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/301)** (A1)
207	- [x] ✅ Cancel run endpoint — gracefully terminate in-progress Celery task — `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` (edit-gated, suite-scoped, 409 if terminal): marks `cancelled` + best-effort `revoke` of the captured `runs.celery_task_id` (migration `d5e6f7a8b9c0`). Cooperative — no SIGKILL mid-GX: worker skips a cancelled run at pickup + `execute_run` won't overwrite a mid-run cancel. Deterministic for queued, best-effort for in-flight. Folded **#227** (one dispatch-failure shape in `run_dispatch`, aligning probe's `finished_at`). **[PR #302](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/302)** (A2)

213	> **Operational result statuses ([#122](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/122) — DONE, closed):** both halves shipped. **`error`** ([PR #297](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/297)) — a check GX can't *evaluate* (per-expectation exception, e.g. a missing column) persists as `error` (no severity, no `metric_value`) instead of being conflated with a data `fail`, never failing its siblings or the run. **`skip`** ([PR #298](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/298), with the flat-file batch run path) — a genuinely-absent batch maps `BatchNotFoundError` → `run_service.skip_run` (every check `skip`, run still succeeds), not a failure. 216	- [x] ✅ Celery beat task — poll ADF REST API every 10 min for **succeeded** runs (skip pipelines updated by webhook recently) — provider-agnostic `poll_orchestration_runs` beat task through the `OrchestrationProvider` seam — [PR #171](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/189)
217	- [x] ✅ Celery beat task — poll Airflow REST API `dagRuns` every 10 min — same provider-agnostic beat task (ADF + Airflow share it) — [PR #171](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/189) _(added per ADR 0004; not in roadmap)_
218	- [x] ✅ Gap recovery logic — on startup + every 30 min, fetch last hour of run statuses — provider-agnostic `recover_orchestration_gaps` beat task reuses the `_poll_orchestration_runs` pipeline with a 1-hour `lookback` (vs the 10-min poll's 15-min window); idempotent upsert + `skip_updated_since` make overlap harmless. New 30-min beat entry + a one-off on worker boot via the `worker_ready` signal (enqueued by name, broker-failure swallowed). **[PR #307](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/307)** (B2)
219	- [x] ✅ `GET /api/v1/orchestration/pipelines` — latest status per pipeline/DAG, provider-agnostic — one row per distinct `(provider, pipeline_or_dag_id, env)` carrying its most-recent run, via Postgres `DISTINCT ON` (no provider branching); auth-only gated (monitoring, not suite-scoped); `provider`/`env`/`limit` filters. Recency = `COALESCE(started_at, created_at)` so a fresh failure event with no start time isn't masked by an older fully-timed run (`/code-review` catch). **[PR #305](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/305)** (B1); follow-up [#306](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/306) (validate provider/env → 422 on both read endpoints)

> AGENT

Here's what's still pending for the **Week 5 exit gate** (*"Async runs with live progress across all datasource types; scheduling operational"*) — **14/19 done, 5 remaining**:

## Backend (9/10 ✅) — 1 left
- ⬜ **Run history retention policy** — configurable purge of results older than N days ([progress.md:208](docs/progress.md#L208))

## Execution UI (1/5 ✅) — 4 left
- ⬜ **Run-now panel** — suite picker, env/datasource, notification target ([progress.md:224](docs/progress.md#L224))
- ⬜ **Live run-progress UI** — check-by-check status with spinner + cancel button ([progress.md:225](docs/progress.md#L225))
- ⬜ **Scheduled-runs table** — create/pause/delete cron schedules ([progress.md:226](docs/progress.md#L226))
- ⬜ **Recent-runs audit table** — with drill-down to results ([progress.md:227](docs/progress.md#L227))

## What's notable

The **backend is essentially complete** — every API the UI needs already exists:
- Live-progress UI → backed by `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (#301) + cancel (#302)
- Scheduled-runs table → backed by the **scheduling backend (A7)** that just merged ([schedules.py CRUD + `dispatch_due_schedules` beat](docs/progress.md#L211))
- Run-now panel → backed by `POST /suites/{id}/run` (PR-C0b)
- Recent-runs → backed by `GET /runs` (PR-C0b)

So **4 of the 5 remaining tasks are pure frontend wiring** over shipped, tested APIs. The only backend gap is the retention purge.

Also worth flagging: the **live warehouse/file run remains the deferred Week-1 smoke** — run paths are unit-tested in-process (real GX on canned DataFrames), but no live Snowflake/ADLS/S3/UC execution has run end-to-end pending credentials.

Want me to pick one of these up — the retention purge backend, or one of the UI panels?

> DEVELOPER

Okay, you decide which one to take up based on priority and dependencies.

> AGENT

Good call. Let me reason through priority + dependencies, then start.

**My pick: the Live run-progress UI.**

Why this one:
- **It's the literal headline of the exit gate** — *"Async runs with **live progress** across all datasource types."* Nothing else moves the gate as directly.
- **Backend is fully shipped and tested** — `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (#301) and `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` (#302) are done. Zero backend dependency.
- **It composes with what already exists** — the manual **Run button** (#245) already ships, but clicking it currently gives no live feedback. Live-progress closes that loop (trigger → watch → cancel).
- **No UI dependency on the other unbuilt panels** — run-now panel, scheduled-runs table, and recent-runs table are all independent of it. Retention purge is backend-isolated and the least gate-relevant.

Dependency-wise the natural UI build order is: live-progress (depends on nothing new) → run-now panel → recent-runs table → scheduled-runs table. So this is also the right *first* piece.

Let me scope it against the actual frontend code before writing anything.

1	import { api } from './client';
2	
3	/**
4	 * Runs / results / pipeline-runs API — the read surface behind the Results page
5	 * (backend `runs.py`, PR-C0b). The DQ-run reads are suite-scoped: the backend
6	 * filters to suites the caller can access, so this client never has to. Manual
7	 * run *triggering* (`runSuite` → `POST /suites/{id}/run`) lives here too, since

10	
11	/** Run execution lifecycle — `status` is execution, not data quality. */
12	export const RUN_STATUSES = ['queued', 'running', 'succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled'] as const;
13	export type RunStatus = (typeof RUN_STATUSES)[number];
14	
15	/** Result severity tier (ADR 0005) + the two operational statuses (#122). */
16	export type ResultStatus = 'pass' | 'warn' | 'fail' | 'critical' | 'skip' | 'error';
17	
18	/** Mirrors the backend `RunRead`. */

20	  id: string;
21	  suite_id: string;
22	  status: RunStatus;
23	  triggered_by: string | null;
24	  started_at: string | null;
25	  finished_at: string | null;
26	  created_at: string;
27	}
28	
29	/** Mirrors `ResultRead` — `sample_failures` is withheld by the API (PII, ADR 0018). */

31	  id: string;
32	  check_id: string;
33	  status: ResultStatus;
34	  metric_value: number | null;
35	  duration_ms: number | null;
36	  observed_value: Record<string, unknown> | null;
37	  expected_value: Record<string, unknown> | null;
38	}
39	
40	/** Mirrors `RunDetailRead` — a run plus its result rows. */

43	}

322	  // Ref guard (not just `running` state) so a synchronous double-click can't

46	// ───────────────────────────── Runs tab ─────────────────────────────
47	
48	function RunsTab() {
49	  // Fetch a page of runs + the accessible suites (for id→name), then filter by
50	  // status client-side — cheap at this volume and avoids a refetch per filter.
51	  const { state } = useAsyncData(() => listRuns({ limit: LIST_LIMIT }));
52	  const { state: suitesState } = useAsyncData(listSuites);
53	  const [status, setStatus] = useState<RunStatus | 'all'>('all');
54	  const [openRun, setOpenRun] = useState<Run | null>(null);
55	
56	  const suiteNames = useMemo(() => {
57	    const map = new Map<string, string>();
58	    if (suitesState.status === 'ok') {
59	      for (const s of suitesState.data) map.set(s.id, s.name);
60	    }
61	    return map;
62	  }, [suitesState]);
63	
64	  if (state.status === 'loading') return <Spin tip="Loading runs…" size="large" />;
65	  if (state.status === 'error') {
66	    return <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load runs" description={state.error} />;
67	  }
68	
69	  const runs = status === 'all' ? state.data : state.data.filter((r) => r.status === status);
70	
71	  const columns: ColumnsType<Run> = [
72	    {
73	      title: 'Suite',
74	      dataIndex: 'suite_id',
75	      render: (suiteId: string) =>
76	        suiteNames.get(suiteId) ?? <Typography.Text code>{suiteId.slice(0, 8)}</Typography.Text>,
77	    },
78	    {
79	      title: 'Status',
80	      dataIndex: 'status',
81	      width: 120,
82	      render: (s: RunStatus) => <Tag color={RUN_STATUS_COLORS[s]}>{s}</Tag>,
83	    },
84	    { title: 'Triggered by', dataIndex: 'triggered_by', render: (t: string | null) => t ?? '—' },
85	    {
86	      title: 'Started',
87	      dataIndex: 'started_at',
88	      render: (t: string | null) => formatTimestamp(t),
89	    },
90	    {
91	      title: 'Duration',
92	      width: 110,
93	      render: (_: unknown, run: Run) => formatDuration(run.started_at, run.finished_at),
94	    },
95	  ];

130	function RunDetailDrawer({

148	          refetches — useAsyncData only fetches on mount/reload, not on prop

155	function RunDetailBody({ run }: { run: Run }) {
156	  // Remounted per run via the `key` at the call site → these fetch fresh.
157	  const { state } = useAsyncData(() => getRun(run.id));
158	  const { state: checksState } = useAsyncData(() => listChecks(run.suite_id));
159	
160	  const checks = useMemo(() => {

162	    if (checksState.status === 'ok') {
163	      for (const c of checksState.data) map.set(c.id, c);

166	  }, [checksState]);
167	
168	  return (

=== useAsyncData ===
import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from 'react';

/** Three-state result of an async fetch. */
export type AsyncState<T> =
  | { status: 'loading' }
  | { status: 'ok'; data: T }
  | { status: 'error'; error: string };

/**
 * Fetch on mount (and on `reload()`), with a cancelled-guard so a late
 * resolution after unmount doesn't set state, and rejection normalised to a
 * string `error`. Shared by the data pages (Home, Connections, …) so the
 * cancelled-effect dance lives in one place rather than being re-derived per page.
 *
 * `reload` re-runs the fetcher (e.g. after a mutation) while keeping the current
 * data visible until the refetch resolves — no flash back to the loading state.
 */
export function useAsyncData<T>(fetcher: () => Promise<T>): {
  state: AsyncState<T>;
  reload: () => void;
} {
  const [state, setState] = useState<AsyncState<T>>({ status: 'loading' });
  const [nonce, setNonce] = useState(0);

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    fetcher()
      .then((data) => {
        if (!cancelled) setState({ status: 'ok', data });
      })
      .catch((err: unknown) => {
        if (!cancelled) {
          setState({ status: 'error', error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) });
        }
      });
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
    // Re-run on mount and whenever `reload` bumps the nonce; the fetcher identity
    // is intentionally not a dependency.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [nonce]);

  const reload = useCallback(() => setNonce((n) => n + 1), []);
  return { state, reload };
}
=== resultsFormat ===
import type { ResultStatus, RunStatus } from '../../api/runs';

/**
 * Pure presentation helpers for the Results surface — kept framework-free so the
 * status→colour mapping and the timing formatters can be unit-tested without
 * rendering antd.
 */

/** antd Tag colour per run execution status. */
export const RUN_STATUS_COLORS: Record<RunStatus, string> = {
  queued: 'default',
  running: 'processing',
  succeeded: 'success',
  failed: 'error',
  cancelled: 'warning',
};

/** antd Tag colour per result severity / operational status (ADR 0005 / #122). */
export const RESULT_STATUS_COLORS: Record<ResultStatus, string> = {
  pass: 'success',
  warn: 'warning',
  fail: 'error',
  critical: 'magenta',
  skip: 'default',
  error: 'volcano',
};

/** Orchestrator pipeline-run status → colour (provider-agnostic value set). */
export function pipelineStatusColor(status: string): string {
  switch (status) {
    case 'succeeded':
      return 'success';
    case 'failed':
      return 'error';
    case 'running':
      return 'processing';
    case 'cancelled':
      return 'warning';
    default:
      return 'default';
  }
}

/**
 * Render an unknown scalar (a GX observed/expected value, a profiled min/max or
 * top value) for display: an em dash for null/undefined, JSON for objects, the
 * `String` form otherwise. Falsy scalars (`0`, `false`, `''`) render as
 * themselves — not collapsed to the em dash — so a real zero isn't mistaken for
 * "no value". The em-dash sentinel matches `formatTimestamp` / `formatDuration`.
 */
export function formatScalar(value: unknown): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return '—';
  return typeof value === 'object' ? JSON.stringify(value) : String(value);
}

/** Absolute timestamp as a locale string, or an em dash when absent. */
export function formatTimestamp(iso: string | null): string {
  if (!iso) return '—';
  const d = new Date(iso);
  return Number.isNaN(d.getTime()) ? '—' : d.toLocaleString();
}

/**
 * Run duration (finished − started) as a compact human string: `850ms`, `12s`,
 * `1m 3s`. Returns an em dash when either bound is missing (queued / never
 * finished) or the interval is negative (clock skew).
 */
export function formatDuration(startedAt: string | null, finishedAt: string | null): string {
  if (!startedAt || !finishedAt) return '—';
  const ms = new Date(finishedAt).getTime() - new Date(startedAt).getTime();
  if (Number.isNaN(ms) || ms < 0) return '—';
  if (ms < 1000) return `${ms}ms`;
  const totalSeconds = Math.round(ms / 1000);
  if (totalSeconds < 60) return `${totalSeconds}s`;
  const minutes = Math.floor(totalSeconds / 60);
  const seconds = totalSeconds % 60;
  return `${minutes}m ${seconds}s`;
}
=== cancel endpoint 193-221 ===
@router.post(
    "/runs/{run_id}/cancel",
    response_model=RunRead,
    summary="Cancel a queued or running run",
)
def cancel_run(
    run_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> RunRead:
    """Cancel a non-terminal run. `edit`-gated (same capability as triggering).

    Marks the run `cancelled` and best-effort revokes its Celery task (dropping it
    if still queued). An already-finished run (succeeded/failed/cancelled) → 409.
    An in-flight run is stopped cooperatively by the worker (it won't overwrite a
    `cancelled` status with results), so cancel may race a fast run to completion.
    """
    run = svc.get_run(db, run_id)
    if run is None:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="run not found")
    # Cancel is a control action on the suite's runs → edit (404 hides the run
    # for a caller who can't see the suite, matching the read endpoints).
    require_permission(db, run.suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="edit")
    if not svc.cancel_run(db, run):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="run is already finished")
    run_dispatch.revoke_run(run.celery_task_id)
    return RunRead.model_validate(run)

=== vite.config.ts ===
import { configDefaults, defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';

const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));

// Proxy target for /api and /mcp. Defaults to localhost:8000 for host-side dev
// (`pnpm dev`); the docker-compose frontend service overrides it to the in-network
// api hostname (http://api:8000), since `localhost` inside the container is the
// frontend container itself.
const apiProxyTarget = process.env.VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET || 'http://localhost:8000';

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  resolve: {
    alias: {
      '@': resolve(__dirname, 'src'),
    },
  },
  server: {
    port: 3000,
    proxy: {
      '/api': apiProxyTarget,
      '/mcp': apiProxyTarget,
    },
  },
  build: {
    rollupOptions: {
      output: {
        // Split the heavy, rarely-changing vendors into their own long-cache
        // chunks so the app chunk stays small and an app change doesn't bust the
        // whole vendor bundle. Routes are additionally lazy-split (App.tsx).
        manualChunks(id: string) {
          if (!id.includes('node_modules')) return undefined;
          if (id.includes('/antd/') || id.includes('@ant-design/')) return 'antd';
          if (id.includes('@azure/')) return 'msal';
          // Monaco is huge (~4 MB) and only used by the custom-SQL editor — keep
          // it in its own chunk so the catch-all `vendor` doesn't pull it eagerly;
          // it's reached only via the React.lazy SqlEditorField (ADR 0019).
          if (id.includes('monaco-editor') || id.includes('@monaco-editor/')) return 'monaco';
          if (
            id.includes('/react/') ||
            id.includes('/react-dom/') ||
            id.includes('/react-router') ||
            id.includes('/scheduler/')
          ) {
            return 'react';
          }
          return 'vendor';
        },
      },
    },
  },
  test: {
    globals: true,
    environment: 'jsdom',
    setupFiles: ['./tests/setup.ts'],
    // Playwright specs live in e2e/ and run under `pnpm e2e`, not Vitest — they
    // import @playwright/test and drive a browser, so keep Vitest out of them.
    exclude: [...configDefaults.exclude, 'e2e/**'],
    coverage: {
      provider: 'v8',
      reporter: ['text', 'lcov'],
      thresholds: {
        lines: 0, // raised to 80 in Week 8
      },
    },
  },
});

=== setup ===
import '@testing-library/jest-dom';

// antd's responsive components (Descriptions, Card, …) call window.matchMedia,
// which jsdom does not implement. Provide a no-op stub so they render in tests.
if (!window.matchMedia) {
  window.matchMedia = (query: string): MediaQueryList =>
    ({
      matches: false,
      media: query,
      onchange: null,
      addListener: () => {},
      removeListener: () => {},
      addEventListener: () => {},
      removeEventListener: () => {},
      dispatchEvent: () => false,
    }) as MediaQueryList;
}

// antd's Select/virtual-list (rc-virtual-list) uses ResizeObserver, absent in
// jsdom. A no-op stub lets dropdown options render in tests.
if (!window.ResizeObserver) {
  window.ResizeObserver = class {
    observe(): void {}
    unobserve(): void {}
    disconnect(): void {}
  };
}

Lighter\n * than the full run+results detail (`getRun`).\n */\nexport interface RunProgress {\n  run_id: string;\n  suite_id: string;\n  status: RunStatus;\n  total_checks: number;\n  completed_checks: number;\n  counts: Record<string, number>;\n  checks: CheckProgress[];\n  started_at: string | null;\n  finished_at: string | null;\n}"

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx",
  "content": "import { App, Alert, Button, Drawer, Empty, Flex, List, Progress, Spin, Tag, Typography } from 'antd';\nimport { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';\nimport { Link } from 'react-router-dom';\n\nimport {\n  cancelRun,\n  getRunProgress,\n  type ResultStatus,\n  type RunProgress,\n  type RunStatus,\n} from '../../api/runs';\nimport { RESULT_STATUS_COLORS, RUN_STATUS_COLORS } from '../results/resultsFormat';\n\n/** Run lifecycle states past which polling stops. */\nconst TERMINAL: readonly RunStatus[] = ['succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled'];\nconst DEFAULT_POLL_MS = 1500;\n\nfunction isTerminal(status: RunStatus): boolean {\n  return TERMINAL.includes(status);\n}\n\n/** antd Progress bar status from the run lifecycle. */\nfunction barStatus(status: RunStatus): 'success' | 'exception' | 'active' | 'normal' {\n  if (status === 'succeeded') return 'success';\n  if (status === 'failed' || status === 'cancelled') return 'exception';\n  if (status === 'running') return 'active';\n  return 'normal';\n}\n\n/**\n * Live run-progress drawer — opens on a queued run and polls\n * `GET /runs/{id}/progress` until the run is terminal, showing the run\n * lifecycle, a completed/total bar, and per-check status (a spinner while a\n * check is still pending). An editor can cancel an in-flight run.\n *\n * Mounted controlled by `runId`; pass `null` to keep it closed. The body is\n * keyed by `runId` so opening a different run remounts and restarts polling\n * rather than continuing the prior run's loop.\n */\nexport function LiveRunProgress({\n  runId,\n  suiteName,\n  canManage,\n  pollMs = DEFAULT_POLL_MS,\n  onClose,\n}: {\n  runId: string | null;\n  suiteName: string | null;\n  canManage: boolean;\n  /** Poll interval; overridable so tests can drive it deterministically. */\n  pollMs?: number;\n  onClose: () => void;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <Drawer\n      open={runId !== null}\n      onClose={onClose}\n      width={560}\n      title={`Run progress${suiteName ? ` · ${suiteName}` : ''}`}\n      destroyOnClose\n    >\n      {runId !== null && (\n        <LiveRunProgressBody key={runId} runId={runId} canManage={canManage} pollMs={pollMs} />\n      )}\n    </Drawer>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction LiveRunProgressBody({\n  runId,\n  canManage,\n  pollMs,\n}: {\n  runId: string;\n  canManage: boolean;\n  pollMs: number;\n}) {\n  const { message } = App.useApp();\n  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<RunProgress | null>(null);\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\n  const [cancelling, setCancelling] = useState(false);\n  const timerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);\n\n  // Poll until terminal via a self-scheduling timeout (not setInterval, so a slow\n  // request can't pile up overlapping fetches). `active` guards a late resolution\n  // after unmount. Polling stops on a terminal status or a fetch error — the\n  // latter surfaces an alert rather than hot-looping a broken endpoint.\n  useEffect(() => {\n    let active = true;\n    const tick = async () => {\n      try {\n        const next = await getRunProgress(runId);\n        if (!active) return;\n        setProgress(next);\n        setError(null);\n        if (!isTerminal(next.status)) {\n          timerRef.current = setTimeout(tick, pollMs);\n        }\n      } catch (err) {\n        if (!active) return;\n        setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));\n      }\n    };\n    void tick();\n    return () => {\n      active = false;\n      if (timerRef.current) clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n    };\n  }, [runId, pollMs]);\n\n  const onCancel = async () => {\n    setCancelling(true);\n    try {\n      const run = await cancelRun(runId);\n      // Reflect the terminal state immediately; the in-flight poll (if any) will\n      // also resolve to it and stop.\n      setProgress((p) => (p ? { ...p, status: run.status, finished_at: run.finished_at } : p));\n      message.success('Run cancelled');\n    } catch (err) {\n      message.error(`Cancel failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);\n    } finally {\n      setCancelling(false);\n    }\n  };\n\n  if (!progress) {\n    if (error) {\n      return (\n        <Alert type=\"error\" showIcon message=\"Failed to load run progress\" description={error} />\n      );\n    }\n    return <Spin tip=\"Starting run…\" size=\"large\" />;\n  }\n\n  const { status, total_checks, completed_checks, checks } = progress;\n  const terminal = isTerminal(status);\n  const percent = total_checks > 0 ? Math.round((completed_checks / total_checks) * 100) : 0;\n\n  return (\n    <Flex vertical gap={16}>\n      <Flex gap={12} align=\"center\" wrap>\n        <Tag color={RUN_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>\n        <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\">\n          {completed_checks} / {total_checks} checks\n        </Typography.Text>\n        {canManage && !terminal && (\n          <Button danger size=\"small\" loading={cancelling} onClick={onCancel}>\n            Cancel\n          </Button>\n        )}\n      </Flex>\n\n      <Progress percent={percent} status={barStatus(status)} />\n\n      {/* A transient poll error while we still have prior progress to show. */}\n      {error && <Alert type=\"warning\" showIcon message=\"Progress update failed\" description={error} />}\n\n      {checks.length === 0 ? (\n        <Empty description=\"This suite has no checks to run.\" />\n      ) : (\n        <List<(typeof checks)[number]>\n          size=\"small\"\n          dataSource={checks}\n          rowKey=\"check_id\"\n          renderItem={(c) => (\n            <List.Item>\n              <Typography.Text>{c.name}</Typography.Text>\n              <CheckStatus status={c.status} />\n            </List.Item>\n          )}\n        />\n      )}\n\n      {terminal && (\n        <Link to=\"/results\">\n          <Typography.Link>View full results →</Typography.Link>\n        </Link>\n      )}\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n\n/** A pending check shows a spinner; a resolved one its severity tag. */\nfunction CheckStatus({ status }: { status: ResultStatus | null }) {\n  if (status === null) {\n    return (\n      <Flex gap={6} align=\"center\">\n        <Spin size=\"small\" />\n        <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\">pending</Typography.Text>\n      </Flex>\n    );\n  }\n  return <Tag color={RESULT_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>;\n}\n"
}

478	        try {
479	          await deleteCheck(suiteId, check.id);
480	          message.success(`${check.name} deleted`);
481	          onChanged();
482	        } catch (err) {
483	          message.error(`Delete failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
484	          throw err; // keep the confirm modal open on failure
485	        }
486	      },
487	    });
488	  };
489	
490	  if (state.status === 'loading') {
491	    return <Spin tip="Loading checks…" />;
492	  }
493	  if (state.status === 'error') {
494	    return <Alert type="error" showIcon title="Failed to load checks" description={state.error} />;
495	  }

  "content": "import { App as AntApp } from 'antd';\nimport { render, screen, waitFor } from '@testing-library/react';\nimport userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';\nimport { MemoryRouter } from 'react-router-dom';\nimport { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';\n\nimport { cancelRun, getRunProgress, type RunProgress, type RunStatus } from '../../src/api/runs';\nimport { LiveRunProgress } from '../../src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress';\n\nvi.mock('../../src/api/runs', async (importOriginal) => {\n  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import('../../src/api/runs')>();\n  return { ...actual, getRunProgress: vi.fn(), cancelRun: vi.fn() };\n});\n\nconst mockProgress = vi.mocked(getRunProgress);\nconst mockCancel = vi.mocked(cancelRun);\n\nfunction progress(status: RunStatus, overrides: Partial<RunProgress> = {}): RunProgress {\n  return {\n    run_id: 'r1',\n    suite_id: 's1',\n    status,\n    total_checks: 2,\n    completed_checks: status === 'succeeded' ? 2 : 1,\n    counts: {},\n    checks: [\n      { check_id: 'c1', name: 'not-null id', status: 'pass' },\n      { check_id: 'c2', name: 'row count', status: status === 'succeeded' ? 'fail' : null },\n    ],\n    started_at: null,\n    finished_at: null,\n    ...overrides,\n  };\n}\n\nfunction renderDrawer(props: Partial<Parameters<typeof LiveRunProgress>[0]> = {}) {\n  return render(\n    <MemoryRouter>\n      <AntApp>\n        <LiveRunProgress\n          runId=\"r1\"\n          suiteName=\"Orders\"\n          canManage\n          pollMs={1_000_000}\n          onClose={() => {}}\n          {...props}\n        />\n      </AntApp>\n    </MemoryRouter>,\n  );\n}\n\nafterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks());\n\ndescribe('LiveRunProgress', () => {\n  it('is closed (no content) when runId is null', () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));\n    renderDrawer({ runId: null });\n    expect(screen.queryByText(/Run progress/)).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('renders per-check status — a pending check spins, a resolved one tags', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));\n    renderDrawer();\n\n    expect(await screen.findByText('not-null id')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.getByText('pass')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    // The unresolved check shows the pending affordance, not a status tag.\n    expect(screen.getByText('pending')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.getByText('1 / 2 checks')).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('polls until the run is terminal, then stops and links to results', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValueOnce(progress('running')).mockResolvedValue(progress('succeeded'));\n    renderDrawer({ pollMs: 5 });\n\n    expect(await screen.findByText('View full results →')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.getByText('succeeded')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    const callsAtTerminal = mockProgress.mock.calls.length;\n    // Give a couple of intervals: no further polling once terminal.\n    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 30));\n    expect(mockProgress.mock.calls.length).toBe(callsAtTerminal);\n  });\n\n  it('cancels an in-flight run for an editor', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));\n    mockCancel.mockResolvedValue({\n      id: 'r1',\n      suite_id: 's1',\n      status: 'cancelled',\n      triggered_by: null,\n      started_at: null,\n      finished_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',\n      created_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',\n    });\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    renderDrawer();\n    await screen.findByText('not-null id');\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' }));\n\n    await waitFor(() => expect(mockCancel).toHaveBeenCalledWith('r1'));\n    // The drawer reflects the terminal state and drops the cancel control.\n    expect(await screen.findByText('cancelled')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('hides the cancel button for a terminal run', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('succeeded'));\n    renderDrawer();\n    await screen.findByText('not-null id');\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('hides the cancel button for a non-editor', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));\n    renderDrawer({ canManage: false });\n    await screen.findByText('not-null id');\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('surfaces a fetch error when there is no progress to show', async () => {\n    mockProgress.mockRejectedValue(new Error('boom'));\n    renderDrawer();\n    expect(await screen.findByText('Failed to load run progress')).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n});\n"
}

  "new_string": "### Execution UI (5 tasks — 2/5 ✅)\n- [ ] ⬜ Run now panel — suite picker, env / datasource, notification target\n- [x] ✅ Live run progress UI — check-by-check status with spinner + cancel button — `LiveRunProgress` drawer (`components/runs/`): opens on the run id returned by the manual **Run** button (#245) instead of bouncing to `/results`, and polls `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (#301) on a self-scheduling timeout (not `setInterval` — no overlapping fetches) until the run is terminal (`succeeded`/`failed`/`cancelled`), then stops. Shows the run-status tag + a completed/total `Progress` bar + a per-check list (a `Spin`+\"pending\" while a check's `status` is null, its severity `Tag` once resolved). An editor (`canRun`) gets a **Cancel** button (`POST /runs/{id}/cancel` #302) on a non-terminal run; cancel reflects the terminal state immediately + the next poll confirms it. On terminal, a \"View full results →\" link to `/results`. Body keyed by run id so opening a new run restarts polling; `pollMs` is injectable for deterministic tests. 7 component tests (pending/resolved render, poll-until-terminal-then-stop, cancel happy path, cancel hidden when terminal / non-editor, fetch-error alert). **(A3)**"
}

Done. Here's what I built and verified.

## Live run-progress UI — shipped on branch `feature/live-run-progress-ui`

**What it does:** clicking the manual **Run** button on a suite now opens a live-progress drawer (instead of bouncing to `/results`) that polls the run check-by-check until it finishes, with a cancel button for editors.

**Changes:**
- [frontend/src/api/runs.ts](frontend/src/api/runs.ts) — added `getRunProgress()` + `cancelRun()` and the `RunProgress`/`CheckProgress` types, mirroring the backend `RunProgressRead`/`CheckProgressRead`.
- [frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx) — the drawer: status tag + completed/total `Progress` bar + per-check list (spinner while a check is `null`/pending, severity tag once resolved), an editor-only **Cancel**, and a "View full results →" link on terminal. Polls on a self-scheduling timeout (no overlapping fetches), stops on terminal/error, guards against post-unmount state.
- [frontend/src/pages/Suites.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Suites.tsx) — `onRun` now opens the drawer on the queued run id.
- [frontend/tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx](frontend/tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx) — 7 tests.

**Verified locally** (per working agreement — not relying on CI as the first loop):
- `tsc --noEmit` clean · ESLint clean · Prettier clean
- Full frontend suite: **206 tests / 30 files pass** (7 new)

**Also synced:** ticked the task in [docs/progress.md](docs/progress.md) (Week 5 → **15/19**, Execution UI 2/5) and the matching item in GitHub epic [#174](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/174) (the PostToolUse hook flagged the mirror).

**Why this leaves the gate cleanly advanced:** of the 4 remaining Week-5 tasks, this was the only one named directly in the exit gate ("live progress"). Remaining: run-now panel, scheduled-runs table (UI), recent-runs table, and the retention purge (backend).

I've **not committed or pushed** — per your workflow, commits/PRs are on your say-so. Want me to commit this (one-functionality commit), and open the PR? Opening the PR is also where I'd kick off `/code-review` per your before-merge rule.

It polls `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (#301) until the run is terminal, showing:\n\n- the run-status tag + a completed/total `Progress` bar\n- a per-check list — a spinner + \"pending\" while a check's status is `null`, its severity `Tag` once resolved\n- an editor-only **Cancel** button (`POST /runs/{id}/cancel` #302) on a non-terminal run\n- a \"View full results →\" link to `/results` once terminal\n\nCloses the Week-5 execution-UI **live run-progress** task (A3). +/**
+ * Poll a run's live progress (`GET /runs/{id}/progress`). Suite-scoped (view).
+ * Cheaper than `getRun` — no observed/expected payloads — so it's the call the
+ * live-progress UI hits on its polling interval.
+ */
+export async function getRunProgress(runId: string): Promise<RunProgress> {
+  const { data } = await api.get<RunProgress>(`/runs/${runId}/progress`);
+  return data;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Cancel a non-terminal run (`POST /runs/{id}/cancel`). Edit-gated; returns the
+ * updated `Run`. An already-finished run → 409. Cancel is cooperative (best-effort
+ * for an in-flight run), so it may race a fast run to completion.
+ */
+export async function cancelRun(runId: string): Promise<Run> {
+  const { data } = await api.post<Run>(`/runs/${runId}/cancel`);
+  return data;
+}

diff --git a/frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx b/frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..ef8a6bf
--- /dev/null
+++ b/frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx
@@ -0,0 +1,204 @@
+import {
+  App,
+  Alert,
+  Button,
+  Drawer,
+  Empty,
+  Flex,
+  List,
+  Progress,
+  Spin,
+  Tag,
+  Typography,
+} from 'antd';
+import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';
+import { Link } from 'react-router-dom';
+
+import {
+  cancelRun,
+  getRunProgress,
+  type ResultStatus,
+  type RunProgress,
+  type RunStatus,
+} from '../../api/runs';
+import { RESULT_STATUS_COLORS, RUN_STATUS_COLORS } from '../results/resultsFormat';
+
+/** Run lifecycle states past which polling stops. */
+const TERMINAL: readonly RunStatus[] = ['succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled'];
+const DEFAULT_POLL_MS = 1500;
+
+function isTerminal(status: RunStatus): boolean {
+  return TERMINAL.includes(status);
+}
+
+/** antd Progress bar status from the run lifecycle. */
+function barStatus(status: RunStatus): 'success' | 'exception' | 'active' | 'normal' {
+  if (status === 'succeeded') return 'success';
+  if (status === 'failed' || status === 'cancelled') return 'exception';
+  if (status === 'running') return 'active';
+  return 'normal';
+}
+
+/**
+ * Live run-progress drawer — opens on a queued run and polls
+ * `GET /runs/{id}/progress` until the run is terminal, showing the run
+ * lifecycle, a completed/total bar, and per-check status (a spinner while a
+ * check is still pending). An editor can cancel an in-flight run.
+ *
+ * Mounted controlled by `runId`; pass `null` to keep it closed. The body is
+ * keyed by `runId` so opening a different run remounts and restarts polling
+ * rather than continuing the prior run's loop.
+ */
+export function LiveRunProgress({
+  runId,
+  suiteName,
+  canManage,
+  pollMs = DEFAULT_POLL_MS,
+  onClose,
+}: {
+  runId: string | null;
+  suiteName: string | null;
+  canManage: boolean;
+  /** Poll interval; overridable so tests can drive it deterministically. */
+  pollMs?: number;
+  onClose: () => void;
+}) {
+  return (
+    <Drawer
+      open={runId !== null}
+      onClose={onClose}
+      width={560}
+      title={`Run progress${suiteName ? ` · ${suiteName}` : ''}`}
+      destroyOnClose
+    >
+      {runId !== null && (
+        <LiveRunProgressBody key={runId} runId={runId} canManage={canManage} pollMs={pollMs} />
+      )}
+    </Drawer>
+  );
+}
+
+function LiveRunProgressBody({
+  runId,
+  canManage,
+  pollMs,
+}: {
+  runId: string;
+  canManage: boolean;
+  pollMs: number;
+}) {
+  const { message } = App.useApp();
+  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<RunProgress | null>(null);
+  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
+  const [cancelling, setCancelling] = useState(false);
+  const timerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
+
+  // Poll until terminal via a self-scheduling timeout (not setInterval, so a slow
+  // request can't pile up overlapping fetches). `active` guards a late resolution
+  // after unmount. Polling stops on a terminal status or a fetch error — the
+  // latter surfaces an alert rather than hot-looping a broken endpoint.
+  useEffect(() => {
+    let active = true;
+    const tick = async () => {
+      try {
+        const next = await getRunProgress(runId);
+        if (!active) return;
+        setProgress(next);
+        setError(null);
+        if (!isTerminal(next.status)) {
+          timerRef.current = setTimeout(tick, pollMs);
+        }
+      } catch (err) {
+        if (!active) return;
+        setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
+      }
+    };
+    void tick();
+    return () => {
+      active = false;
+      if (timerRef.current) clearTimeout(timerRef.current);
+    };
+  }, [runId, pollMs]);
+
+  const onCancel = async () => {
+    setCancelling(true);
+    try {
+      const run = await cancelRun(runId);
+      // Reflect the terminal state immediately; the in-flight poll (if any) will
+      // also resolve to it and stop.
+      setProgress((p) => (p ? { ...p, status: run.status, finished_at: run.finished_at } : p));
+      message.success('Run cancelled');
+    } catch (err) {
+      message.error(`Cancel failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
+    } finally {
+      setCancelling(false);
+    }
+  };
+
+  if (!progress) {
+    if (error) {
+      return (
+        <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load run progress" description={error} />
+      );
+    }
+    return <Spin tip="Starting run…" size="large" />;
+  }
+
+  const { status, total_checks, completed_checks, checks } = progress;
+  const terminal = isTerminal(status);
+  const percent = total_checks > 0 ? Math.round((completed_checks / total_checks) * 100) : 0;
+
+  return (
+    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
+      <Flex gap={12} align="center" wrap>
+        <Tag color={RUN_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>
+        <Typography.Text type="secondary">
+          {completed_checks} / {total_checks} checks
+        </Typography.Text>
+        {canManage && !terminal && (
+          <Button danger size="small" loading={cancelling} onClick={onCancel}>
+            Cancel
+          </Button>
+        )}
+      </Flex>
+
+      <Progress percent={percent} status={barStatus(status)} />
+
+      {/* A transient poll error while we still have prior progress to show. */}
+      {error && (
+        <Alert type="warning" showIcon message="Progress update failed" description={error} />
+      )}
+
+      {checks.length === 0 ? (
+        <Empty description="This suite has no checks to run." />
+      ) : (
+        <List<(typeof checks)[number]>
+          size="small"
+          dataSource={checks}
+          rowKey="check_id"
+          renderItem={(c) => (
+            <List.Item>
+              <Typography.Text>{c.name}</Typography.Text>
+              <CheckStatus status={c.status} />
+            </List.Item>
+          )}
+        />
+      )}
+
+      {terminal && <Link to="/results">View full results →</Link>}
+    </Flex>
+  );
+}
+
+/** A pending check shows a spinner; a resolved one its severity tag. */
+function CheckStatus({ status }: { status: ResultStatus | null }) {
+  if (status === null) {
+    return (
+      <Flex gap={6} align="center">
+        <Spin size="small" />
+        <Typography.Text type="secondary">pending</Typography.Text>
+      </Flex>
+    );
+  }
+  return <Tag color={RESULT_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>;
+}

@@ -327,9 +330,11 @@ function SuiteDetail({
     runningRef.current = true;

-      await runSuite(suite.id);

+      setProgressRunId(run.id);
     } catch (err) {
       message.error(`Run failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
     } finally {
@@ -450,6 +455,12 @@ function SuiteDetail({
         canManage={canManage}
         onClose={() => setShareOpen(false)}
       />
+      <LiveRunProgress
+        runId={progressRunId}

diff --git a/frontend/tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx b/frontend/tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..07f4c1b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/frontend/tests/components/LiveRunProgress.test.tsx
@@ -0,0 +1,129 @@
+import { App as AntApp } from 'antd';
+import { render, screen, waitFor } from '@testing-library/react';
+import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';
+import { MemoryRouter } from 'react-router-dom';
+import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
+
+import { cancelRun, getRunProgress, type RunProgress, type RunStatus } from '../../src/api/runs';
+import { LiveRunProgress } from '../../src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress';
+
+vi.mock('../../src/api/runs', async (importOriginal) => {
+  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import('../../src/api/runs')>();
+  return { ...actual, getRunProgress: vi.fn(), cancelRun: vi.fn() };
+});
+
+const mockProgress = vi.mocked(getRunProgress);
+const mockCancel = vi.mocked(cancelRun);
+
+function progress(status: RunStatus, overrides: Partial<RunProgress> = {}): RunProgress {
+  return {
+    run_id: 'r1',
+    suite_id: 's1',
+    status,
+    total_checks: 2,
+    completed_checks: status === 'succeeded' ? 2 : 1,
+    counts: {},
+    checks: [
+      { check_id: 'c1', name: 'not-null id', status: 'pass' },
+      { check_id: 'c2', name: 'row count', status: status === 'succeeded' ? 'fail' : null },
+    ],
+    started_at: null,
+    finished_at: null,
+    ...overrides,
+  };
+}
+
+function renderDrawer(props: Partial<Parameters<typeof LiveRunProgress>[0]> = {}) {
+  return render(
+    <MemoryRouter>
+      <AntApp>
+        <LiveRunProgress
+          runId="r1"
+          suiteName="Orders"
+          canManage
+          pollMs={1_000_000}
+          onClose={() => {}}
+          {...props}
+        />
+      </AntApp>
+    </MemoryRouter>,
+  );
+}
+
+afterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks());
+
+describe('LiveRunProgress', () => {
+  it('is closed (no content) when runId is null', () => {
+    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));
+    renderDrawer({ runId: null });
+    expect(screen.queryByText(/Run progress/)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('renders per-check status — a pending check spins, a resolved one tags', async () => {
+    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));
+    renderDrawer();
+
+    expect(await screen.findByText('not-null id')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.getByText('pass')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    // The unresolved check shows the pending affordance, not a status tag.
+    expect(screen.getByText('pending')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.getByText('1 / 2 checks')).toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('polls until the run is terminal, then stops and links to results', async () => {
+    mockProgress
+      .mockResolvedValueOnce(progress('running'))
+      .mockResolvedValue(progress('succeeded'));
+    renderDrawer({ pollMs: 5 });
+
+    expect(await screen.findByText('View full results →')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.getByText('succeeded')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    const callsAtTerminal = mockProgress.mock.calls.length;
+    // Give a couple of intervals: no further polling once terminal.
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 30));
+    expect(mockProgress.mock.calls.length).toBe(callsAtTerminal);
+  });
+
+  it('cancels an in-flight run for an editor', async () => {
+    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));
+    mockCancel.mockResolvedValue({
+      id: 'r1',
+      suite_id: 's1',
+      status: 'cancelled',
+      triggered_by: null,
+      started_at: null,
+      finished_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',
+      created_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',
+    });
+    const user = userEvent.setup();
+    renderDrawer();
+    await screen.findByText('not-null id');
+
+    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' }));
+
+    await waitFor(() => expect(mockCancel).toHaveBeenCalledWith('r1'));
+    // The drawer reflects the terminal state and drops the cancel control.
+    expect(await screen.findByText('cancelled')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('hides the cancel button for a terminal run', async () => {
+    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('succeeded'));
+    renderDrawer();
+    await screen.findByText('not-null id');
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('hides the cancel button for a non-editor', async () => {
+    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));
+    renderDrawer({ canManage: false });
+    await screen.findByText('not-null id');
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('surfaces a fetch error when there is no progress to show', async () => {
+    mockProgress.mockRejectedValue(new Error('boom'));
+    renderDrawer();
+    expect(await screen.findByText('Failed to load run progress')).toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+});
```

Finding #2 is the structural root cause of #1 (cleanup/`active` does not cover the cancel path and the in-flight fetch is never aborted). Finding #3 (transient-error stops live polling permanently) is also concrete and independent of the race.

Now I have full context. Let me analyze the cancel→poll race claim precisely.

The cancel handler does `setProgress((p) => ({ ...p, status: run.status, finished_at }))`. But the in-flight poll's `tick()` already scheduled the next `setTimeout` BEFORE cancel — actually no. Let me trace: when `tick` resolves with a non-terminal status, it schedules the next timeout. The cancel optimistically sets status to terminal but does NOT clear `timerRef`. So a pending `setTimeout(tick)` (scheduled by the last non-terminal poll) will still fire. That next `tick` calls `getRunProgress` again. If the backend hasn't yet committed the cancel, it could return `running`, and `setProgress(next)` would OVERWRITE the terminal status back to `running` — and reschedule polling. This is exactly the race the progress.md claims is handled ("the next poll confirms it").

Let me verify the timer is not cleared on cancel.

The cancel handler never touches `timerRef` or `active`. So yes — there's a live scheduled poll that can clobber the optimistic terminal state if the backend cancel hasn't propagated yet. The progress.md claim "the next poll confirms it" assumes the backend immediately reflects cancelled, which for a cooperative/best-effort cancel (the cancelRun docstring says "cooperative... may race a fast run to completion") is not guaranteed.

Let me confirm there's no cleanup of the timer in onCancel.

The code is fully visible. `onCancel` only does setProgress + message. The timer keeps running. This is a genuine finding for both ANGLE 1 (the optimistic patch is a bandaid that can be overwritten) and the progress.md accuracy check.

Here are my findings.

The cleaner depth would be to abort/clear the pending timer in onCancel (or set a `cancelled` guard) rather than rely on an optimistic state patch the next poll can reverse."

Summary of the review:

Strongest finding (ANGLE 1 + the progress.md accuracy check converge): the **cancel→stale-poll race is not actually closed**. `onCancel` optimistically patches `setProgress` to the terminal status but never clears the already-scheduled `setTimeout(tick)`. Since `cancelRun` is documented as cooperative/best-effort, the next poll can read `running` from the backend and overwrite the optimistic `cancelled` back to `running` while rescheduling polling. The progress.md claim "cancel reflects the terminal state immediately + the next poll confirms it" overstates this — the next poll can *contradict* it, not confirm it. A timer-abort (clear `timerRef` / set a guard) in `onCancel` would be the non-bandaid fix.

Secondary structural concern: the poll loop is built inline despite an existing `useAsyncData` hook that centralizes the same cancelled-guard/error-normalize pattern, and 2+ more polling consumers (recent-runs, scheduled-runs) are queued for the same week.

Convention checks that **passed** (no violation to flag): single conventional commit `feat(execution): live run-progress drawer (A3)` is correctly scoped/typed; the PR ships tests; the progress.md "7 component tests" claim is **accurate** (exactly 7 `it(` blocks).

Summary: The reuse angles flagged in the brief largely check out as already-handled by the author — `useAsyncData` is genuinely one-shot (confirmed by its own docstring) so the polling loop legitimately can't reuse it, and `RESULT_STATUS_COLORS`/`RUN_STATUS_COLORS` are correctly imported from `resultsFormat.ts` rather than re-defined. `destroyOnClose` + the `runId !== null` body gate do unmount the body and stop polling on close, so there's no leaked-poll efficiency bug.

The real cleanups: (1) `counts` is dead in the component despite the type/docstring advertising a histogram; (2) a transient poll error permanently strands a live run because the catch doesn't reschedule; (3) the status→Tag JSX is now duplicated a 4th/5th time with no shared `StatusTag`; (4) `barStatus` is a parallel hand-switch that belongs next to `RUN_STATUS_COLORS`; (5) the optimistic cancel merge is derivable from the next poll.

    checks: list[CheckProgress]


def get_run_progress(session: Session, run: Run) -> RunProgress:
    """Assemble a run's progress from the suite's checks + the run's results.

    DB-driven (not Celery task state): the worker writes the ``run.status``
    lifecycle (queued → running → succeeded/failed/cancelled) and the per-check
    ``Result`` rows, so the DB is the source of truth and this composes with the
    same suite-scoped authz the rest of the read API uses.

    Each suite check maps to its result's status, or ``None`` while pending.
    Note: because GX validates a suite in one atomic batch, all result rows land
    together at completion — so mid-run a check reads ``pending`` and the
    histogram fills at the terminal transition (this endpoint reports lifecycle +
    final per-check resolution, not sub-GX incremental progress). Checks are taken
    from the *current* suite definition; a result is matched to its check by id.
    """
    checks = list(
        session.scalars(
            select(Check).where(Check.suite_id == run.suite_id).order_by(Check.created_at)
        )
    )
    # One result per (run_id, check_id) in v1 (each run writes one row per check);
    # keyed by check_id to join against the suite's current checks.
    results = {r.check_id: r for r in list_results(session, run.id)}
    counts: dict[str, int] = dict.fromkeys(RESULT_STATUSES, 0)
    per_check: list[CheckProgress] = []
    completed = 0
    for check in checks:
        result = results.get(check.id)
        status = result.status if result is not None else None
        per_check.append(CheckProgress(check_id=check.id, name=check.name, status=status))
        if status is not None:
            completed += 1
            counts[status] = counts.get(status, 0) + 1
    return RunProgress(
        run=run,
        total_checks=len(checks),
        completed_checks=completed,
        counts=counts,
        checks=per_check,
    )

> AGENT

Verification complete. Backend `get_run_progress` confirms several: `completed` counts only checks that have a result row, and GX writes all rows atomically — so a cancelled/failed run with no rows yields all-`null` check statuses. The existing `Suites.test.tsx` (line 35) re-mocks `api/runs` with **only** `runSuite`, so the new drawer's `getRunProgress` is `undefined` there.

Here's the review of PR #315.

## Code review — `feature/live-run-progress-ui`

### Findings (ranked)

**1. Cancel can flicker back to "running" — the in-flight poll isn't cancelled** · [LiveRunProgress.tsx:123-136](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L123-L136)
`onCancel` optimistically patches status to terminal but never clears `timerRef` or sets an ignore-guard. A poll that was already dispatched *before* the click (reading the run pre-commit) resolves *after* `onCancel`, calls `setProgress(next='running')`, and reschedules — so the status tag flips back to `running` and the Cancel button reappears for up to one `pollMs` before the next tick self-corrects to `cancelled`. The progress.md claim "the next poll confirms it" is the optimistic case; the in-flight poll can momentarily contradict it. **Fix:** in `onCancel`, `clearTimeout(timerRef.current)` and guard the poll's `setProgress` from downgrading an already-terminal status.

**2. A terminal run with no result rows spins forever** · [LiveRunProgress.tsx:198-207](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L198-L207)
`CheckStatus` renders a spinner + "pending" whenever `status === null`. Backend confirms a cancelled (or rows-less failed) run leaves every check `null`, and polling has stopped (terminal). Result: a cancelled run shows all checks spinning "pending" permanently. **Fix:** when the run is terminal, render `null`-status checks as a neutral "not run"/"—" rather than a live spinner (pass `terminal` down to `CheckStatus`).

**3. Dismissing the drawer mid-run strands the run** · [Suites.tsx:333-337](frontend/src/pages/Suites.tsx#L333-L337)
The old flow `navigate('/results')` always dropped the user on the persistent runs table. Now the drawer is the only surface, the "View full results →" link renders **only when terminal** ([LiveRunProgress.tsx:188](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L188)), and the suite panel has no recent-runs list. Close the drawer before the run finishes → no in-app path back to it. **Fix:** show the `/results` link unconditionally (not just on terminal), or keep a linked toast.

**4. One transient poll error kills live updates for the rest of the run** · [LiveRunProgress.tsx:111-114](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L111-L114)
The `catch` sets `error` but never reschedules, so a single 502/network blip mid-run freezes the bar until the drawer is reopened. The comment frames stop-on-error as deliberate — reasonable for a hard-down endpoint, but it conflates transient with permanent. **Fix:** if the last-known status is non-terminal, reschedule on error (optionally with a bounded failure counter).

**5. The manual-run test masks the new drawer path** · [Suites.test.tsx:35](frontend/tests/pages/Suites.test.tsx#L35)
`vi.mock('../../src/api/runs', () => ({ runSuite: vi.fn() }))` omits `getRunProgress`/`cancelRun`, so clicking Run now silently hits the drawer's error path (calling `undefined`) while the test asserts only that `runSuite` was called. The new behavior is uncovered. **Fix:** add `getRunProgress: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(...)` to the mock and assert the drawer opens.

**6. `counts` histogram is dead in the UI** · [LiveRunProgress.tsx:147](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L147)
`RunProgress.counts` is populated by the backend and the [runs.ts docstring](frontend/src/api/runs.ts#L92) advertises "a status histogram… the UI polls," but the component never renders it. Either render a tag row from `Object.entries(counts)` or drop the claim. Low.

**7. `barStatus` belongs with the other run-status presentation** · [LiveRunProgress.tsx:35-40](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx#L35-L40)
It's a parallel hand-maintained switch over `RunStatus`, living apart from `RUN_STATUS_COLORS` in [resultsFormat.ts](frontend/src/components/results/resultsFormat.ts) (which exists precisely to be the framework-free, unit-tested home for these mappings). Low cleanup.

### Note (pre-existing, not this PR)
Because GX validates a suite as one atomic batch, `completed_checks` stays `0` until the very end, then jumps to total — the "check-by-check" bar is effectively binary 0→100% for any non-trivial suite. Documented in the backend, but worth knowing the live granularity is lifecycle-level, not per-check.

---

My recommendation: fix **1–4** in this PR (they're real UX bugs in the headline feature), fold in **5** since it's the same files, and either fix or file **6–7** as follow-ups (per your "deferred findings never silently dropped" rule).

81	function LiveRunProgressBody({
82	  runId,
83	  canManage,

90	  const { message } = App.useApp();
91	  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<RunProgress | null>(null);
92	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
93	  const [cancelling, setCancelling] = useState(false);
94	  const timerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
95	
96	  // Poll until terminal via a self-scheduling timeout (not setInterval, so a slow
97	  // request can't pile up overlapping fetches). `active` guards a late resolution
98	  // after unmount. Polling stops on a terminal status or a fetch error — the
99	  // latter surfaces an alert rather than hot-looping a broken endpoint.
100	  useEffect(() => {
101	    let active = true;
102	    const tick = async () => {
103	      try {
104	        const next = await getRunProgress(runId);
105	        if (!active) return;
106	        setProgress(next);
107	        setError(null);

109	          timerRef.current = setTimeout(tick, pollMs);

113	        setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));

118	      active = false;
119	      if (timerRef.current) clearTimeout(timerRef.current);
120	    };
121	  }, [runId, pollMs]);

124	    setCancelling(true);
125	    try {

129	      setProgress((p) => (p ? { ...p, status: run.status, finished_at: run.finished_at } : p));
130	      message.success('Run cancelled');

  "new_string": "          renderItem={(c) => (\n            <List.Item>\n              <Typography.Text>{c.name}</Typography.Text>\n              <CheckStatus status={c.status} terminal={terminal} />\n            </List.Item>\n          )}\n        />\n      )}\n\n      {/* Always offer the persistent results surface — the drawer can be closed\n          mid-run, and (unlike the old navigate-on-run) it's the only in-app path\n          back to this run until the recent-runs table lands. */}\n      <Link to=\"/results\">View full results →</Link>\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * A check's status cell: a resolved check shows its severity tag; a pending\n * check spins *while the run is live*, but on a terminal run (a check that never\n * produced a result — e.g. a cancelled run) it shows a neutral \"not run\" rather\n * than an eternal spinner.\n */\nfunction CheckStatus({ status, terminal }: { status: ResultStatus | null; terminal: boolean }) {\n  if (status === null) {\n    if (terminal) {\n      return <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\">not run</Typography.Text>;\n    }\n    return (\n      <Flex gap={6} align=\"center\">\n        <Spin size=\"small\" />\n        <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\">pending</Typography.Text>\n      </Flex>\n    );\n  }\n  return <Tag color={RESULT_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>;\n}"
}

  "new_string": "    await waitFor(() => expect(mockCancel).toHaveBeenCalledWith('r1'));\n    // The drawer reflects the terminal state and drops the cancel control.\n    expect(await screen.findByText('cancelled')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n    // A never-run check on a now-terminal run reads \"not run\", not a live spinner.\n    expect(screen.getByText('not run')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.queryByText('pending')).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('does not flip back to running when a late poll resolves after a cancel', async () => {\n    // Backend cancel is cooperative — a poll already in flight can still read\n    // `running`. The cancelled state must stick and polling must stop.\n    mockProgress.mockResolvedValue(progress('running'));\n    mockCancel.mockResolvedValue({\n      id: 'r1',\n      suite_id: 's1',\n      status: 'cancelled',\n      triggered_by: null,\n      started_at: null,\n      finished_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',\n      created_at: '2026-06-20T00:00:00Z',\n    });\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    renderDrawer({ pollMs: 5 });\n    await screen.findByText('not-null id');\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Cancel' }));\n    await screen.findByText('cancelled');\n\n    const callsAtCancel = mockProgress.mock.calls.length;\n    // Let any in-flight / would-be-scheduled polls settle.\n    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 40));\n    expect(screen.getByText('cancelled')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.queryByText('running')).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n    // Polling has stopped — no further progress fetches after the cancel.\n    expect(mockProgress.mock.calls.length).toBe(callsAtCancel);\n  });\n\n  it('recovers from a transient poll error and keeps polling', async () => {\n    mockProgress\n      .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('blip'))\n      .mockResolvedValue(progress('succeeded'));\n    renderDrawer({ pollMs: 5 });\n\n    // The first poll failed, but polling self-heals to the terminal state.\n    expect(await screen.findByText('succeeded')).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });"
}

{
  "command": "cd /Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ\ngh issue create --title \"Run progress is binary 0%→100% (GX atomic batch) — no true per-check incremental progress\" \\\n  --label enhancement --label \"priority/P3\" \\\n  --milestone \"Backlog (post-v1 / testing)\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nSurfaced during the /code-review of PR #315 (live run-progress drawer, A3). Pre-existing limitation, not introduced by that PR — filing so it isn't lost.\n\n`run_service.get_run_progress` derives `completed_checks`/`counts` from the presence of `Result` rows. Because GX validates a suite in **one atomic batch**, all result rows land together at completion — so for any non-trivial suite the live-progress bar reads `0 / N` (0%) for the entire run, then jumps to `N / N` (100%) at the terminal transition. The endpoint reports **lifecycle + final per-check resolution**, not sub-GX incremental progress (documented in the `get_run_progress` docstring).\n\nThis contradicts the UX intent the consuming code advertises (\"watches it execute check-by-check\" — [Suites.tsx](frontend/src/pages/Suites.tsx) onRun comment): a long run will read as hung at 0% until it finishes.\n\n**Options (all post-v1, non-trivial):**\n- Per-check execution (split the GX batch / run validations incrementally) so rows land as checks complete — biggest change, touches the runner.\n- A spinner-only \"running\" affordance for multi-check suites instead of a 0% bar, to avoid the \"hung\" read.\n- Surface an elapsed-time / heartbeat instead of a misleading percentage while `completed_checks == 0`.\n\nTie-in: the `freshness`/`volume` monitor-kinds (ADR 0012) and any non-GX runner may not be atomic-batch, so a per-runner progress contract is the deeper fix.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -1",
  "description": "File the note (binary progress limitation)"
}
```

=== #310 ===
decision (ADR): history/audit strategy — check-version history lost on delete, no audit log, no soft-delete
ms=Backlog (post-v1 / testing)
---
## Context

The internal Postgres data has **no general SCD/history framework** — it's Alembic for schema + targeted mechanisms. The history/audit review found:

1. **Check version history is lost on delete.** `check_versions.check_id` is `ondelete=CASCADE`, so deleting a check (or a suite, which cascades to its checks) wipes its entire `check_versions` history. History survives *edits* (SCD Type 4) but not *deletion*.
2. **No general audit log.** `connections` (credential/config changes), `shares` (grant/revoke), `trigger_bindings` are SCD Type 1 (overwrite + `updated_at`) or hard-deleted with no who/when/before/after trail.
3. **All deletes are hard deletes** (`session.delete`) — no soft-delete (`deleted_at`) anywhere. A deleted connection orphans the meaning of past runs ("what did this run actually check?").
4. **No SCD Type 2** (`valid_from`/`valid_to` versioned dimensions) anywhere.

## Decision needed (ADR)

Pick the history/audit posture for v1.x+:
- **(a)** Preserve check history on delete — `check_versions.check_id` → `RESTRICT`/`SET NULL` + self-contained identity, or a tombstone.
- **(b)** Soft-delete (`deleted_at`) for `connections`/`suites` so run lineage survives.
- **(c)** A single cross-entity **audit log** (`actor, entity_type, entity_id, action, before, after, ts`) — cheaper/uniform vs per-table versioning; covers connections/shares/triggers that have no history today.
- **(d)** Full SCD Type 2 on selected dimensions (heaviest — see the cost analysis on this issue).

## Acceptance
- [ ] ADR recording the chosen posture + rationale + which entities get which treatment
- [ ] Follow-up implementation issue(s) per the decision (e.g. the `check_versions` ondelete fix is small and could be pulled forward)

## Context
From the history/SCD review. Single-tenant v1 likely doesn't need full SCD2, but the check-history-on-delete (1) is a near-term correctness item worth deciding deliberately.


=== ADR 0020 exists? ===
0020-history-and-audit-strategy.md
```

```
1	# ADR 0020 — History/audit strategy: per-entity Type-4 snapshot tables, no SCD-2
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-06-20
5	- **Deciders:** @TheurgicDuke771
6	- **Related:** ADR [0009](0009-flat-monorepo-layout.md), [0010](0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md) (least-privilege / secrets in the SecretStore), [0013](0013-marketplace-distribution-and-anti-lock-in.md) (BYOL portability); issues [#310](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/310) (this decision), [#308](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/308)/[#309](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/309) (the consistency-hardening follow-ups surfaced alongside it)
7	
8	## Context
9	
10	The internal Postgres data has no general history/versioning framework. **Alembic versions the *schema* (DDL), not the *data*** — it cannot answer "what was this connection's config last Tuesday." A consistency/SCD review (2026-06-20) found:
11	
12	- **One real history mechanism exists:** `check_versions` — an immutable snapshot of a check's editable state written on create + every successful update (effectively **SCD Type 4**, a separate history table). It backs the check version-history drawer.
13	- Everything else is **SCD Type 1** (overwrite + `updated_at`): `connections` (config/credential changes), `suites`, `trigger_bindings`, `shares`. No prior-value retention.
14	- **All deletes are hard deletes**; no soft-delete (`deleted_at`) anywhere; no **SCD Type 2** (`valid_from`/`valid_to` versioned rows) anywhere.
15	
16	The question: what history/audit posture should v1.x take, and specifically — do we adopt SCD Type 2?
17	
18	**Why SCD-2 is a poor fit here.** This is a richly-linked OLTP graph (`suites→connections`, `checks→suites`, `runs→suites`, `results→runs/checks`, `shares`, `trigger_bindings`, the `*_versions` tables). True SCD-2 makes the entity id non-unique (one row per version), which breaks every foreign key that points at it. The only way to get Type-2 semantics without destroying the FK model is a *separate* history table per entity — which is **Type 4**, i.e. exactly what `check_versions` already is. Full SCD-2's cost is dominated by **ongoing maintenance**, not the build: every read needs a temporal predicate (`valid_to IS NULL`) or a current-only view, every new column must be mirrored, history grows unbounded (retention/partitioning), and the close-then-insert write adds its own concurrency surface. That tax is unjustified for a single-tenant DQ platform.
19	
20	## Decision
21	
22	1. **No SCD Type 2.** Reject versioned-row dimensions; they fight the FK model and carry a permanent maintenance tax.
23	2. **Per-entity Type-4 snapshot tables where config history has a concrete need.** Extend the `check_versions` pattern entity-by-entity rather than build a framework. **`connection_versions` is added now** (the first such extension beyond checks) — connection name/config history, snapshot on create + name/config update.
24	3. **Credentials are never snapshotted.** Secrets live only in the SecretStore (referenced by the constant `conn-<id>` pointer); history tables hold only editable, **non-secret** fields. A credential rotation (`reauth`, or a secret-only update) records **no** version — that is an audit-log concern, not config history (ADR 0010).
25	4. **Cascade-delete is accepted: history is not retained past entity deletion.** Both `check_versions` and `connection_versions` use `ondelete=CASCADE` on the entity FK; deleting the entity drops its history. (`changed_by` is `SET NULL` so a snapshot outlives its *author*, just not its entity.) Retention-past-delete would need a tombstone/soft-delete; explicitly **out of scope** for v1 — revisit only if an audit/compliance requirement appears.
26	5. **No soft-delete in v1.** Hard deletes stay. (A deleted connection orphaning the *meaning* of past runs is a known, accepted limitation.)
27	6. **A cross-entity audit log (`actor, entity, action, before/after, ts`) is deferred, not rejected.** It is the right tool if/when "who changed this credential/share, and to what" becomes a requirement — cheaper and more uniform than per-entity versioning for *audit* (vs *config history*). Tracked in #310.
28	
29	## Consequences
30	
31	**Positive**
32	- Smallest viable surface: history is additive Type-4 tables (backward-compatible migrations, no FK redesign, no read-path rewrite), reusing a proven pattern + UI shape.
33	- Credentials provably stay out of history; the snapshot is safe to expose through the read API.
34	- Portable for BYOL (ADR 0013): plain tables, no Postgres-temporal or extension dependency.
35	
36	**Negative / watch**
37	- History is **lost on delete** by design — if compliance later needs retention-past-delete, revisit (4).
38	- Per-entity history means **N decisions/migrations**, one per entity that needs it (checks ✅, connections ✅; suites/triggers not yet) — deliberate, to avoid a premature framework.
39	- No unified audit trail yet; credential-rotation events in particular are unrecorded until the audit log lands (#310).
40	
41	## Alternatives considered
42	
43	- **Full SCD-2 (versioned rows or a library like SQLAlchemy-Continuum):** rejected — FK-model break + permanent query/maintenance tax, disproportionate for single-tenant v1.
44	- **Generic audit log first:** strong option (one hook, covers everything) and still on the table for *audit*; but it gives before/after events, not the first-class "version history" view the product already surfaces for checks (and now connections). The two are complementary — Type-4 for config history now, audit log when audit is required.
45	- **Soft-delete everywhere:** deferred — adds a `deleted_at IS NULL` predicate to every read for a need v1 doesn't have.
46	
```

#310 is **resolved by ADR 0020** — let me confirm the mapping and act on it, while I start scoping the next item.

ADR 0020 directly answers every decision point in #310:
- **SCD-2** → rejected (§Decision 1)
- **check_versions lost on delete** (#310's "near-term correctness item") → cascade-delete **deliberately accepted** as v1 behavior (§4), not a bug to fix
- **soft-delete** → deferred (§5)
- **cross-entity audit log** → "deferred, not rejected… **Tracked in #310**" (§6)

So the *decision* half is done; what's left in #310 is the **deferred post-v1 audit log**, which ADR 0020 consciously punted until an audit/compliance requirement appears. **Verdict: not one to take up now** — it'd be building something the ADR just decided to defer. I'll re-scope the issue to reflect that it's now an audit-log tracker, not an open decision.
