=== schedules API ===
"""Schedule CRUD endpoints — manage cron-driven suite run schedules (A7).

Thin HTTP layer over `schedule_service`: a schedule fires a suite run on a cron
cadence (`cron` + `timezone` → `suite_id`). All validation (cron / timezone /
suite-permission) and `next_run_at` bookkeeping live in the service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import uuid
from datetime import datetime
from typing import Annotated

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, status
from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field
from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

from backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user
from backend.app.db.models import Schedule, User
from backend.app.db.session import get_db
from backend.app.services import schedule_service as svc

router = APIRouter(tags=["schedules"])


class ScheduleCreate(BaseModel):
    suite_id: uuid.UUID
    cron: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=128)
    timezone: str = Field(default="UTC", min_length=1, max_length=64)
    enabled: bool = True


class ScheduleUpdate(BaseModel):
    """Partial update — only the supplied fields change. `next_run_at` is
    recomputed by the service when the cadence changes or a paused schedule is
    re-enabled."""

    cron: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=128)
    timezone: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=64)
    enabled: bool | None = None


class ScheduleRead(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)

    id: uuid.UUID
    suite_id: uuid.UUID
    cron: str
    timezone: str
    enabled: bool
    next_run_at: datetime
    last_run_at: datetime | None


@router.post(
    "/schedules",
    response_model=ScheduleRead,
    status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED,
    summary="Create a suite run schedule",
)
def create_schedule(
    payload: ScheduleCreate,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> Schedule:
    return svc.create_schedule(
        db,
        suite_id=payload.suite_id,
        cron_expr=payload.cron,
        timezone=payload.timezone,
        enabled=payload.enabled,
        user_id=current_user.id,
    )


@router.get(
    "/schedules",
    response_model=list[ScheduleRead],
    summary="List schedules on accessible suites",
)
def list_schedules(
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,
    enabled: bool | None = None,
) -> list[Schedule]:
    return svc.list_schedules(db, user_id=current_user.id, suite_id=suite_id, enabled=enabled)


@router.get(
    "/schedules/{schedule_id}",
    response_model=ScheduleRead,
    summary="Get a schedule",
)
def get_schedule(
    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> Schedule:
    return svc.get_schedule(db, schedule_id, user_id=current_user.id)


@router.patch(
    "/schedules/{schedule_id}",
    response_model=ScheduleRead,
    summary="Update a schedule (cron / timezone / enabled)",
)
def update_schedule(
    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,
    payload: ScheduleUpdate,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> Schedule:
    return svc.update_schedule(
        db,
        schedule_id,
        user_id=current_user.id,
        cron_expr=payload.cron,
        timezone=payload.timezone,
        enabled=payload.enabled,
    )


@router.delete(
    "/schedules/{schedule_id}",
    status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT,
    summary="Delete a schedule",
)
def delete_schedule(
    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,
    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
) -> None:
    svc.delete_schedule(db, schedule_id, user_id=current_user.id)
```

=== triggerBindings.ts ===
import { api } from './client';

/**
 * Trigger bindings — map a successful orchestrator run to a suite so the suite
 * runs on that pipeline/DAG's success (CLAUDE.md §4). Provider-agnostic: the
 * composite key (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) → `suite_id`. Managing
 * a binding needs `edit` on the suite (backend-gated); listing needs `view`.
 * Orchestration providers are *never* a datasource — this is the only place a
 * pipeline/DAG id is bound to a suite.
 */

export const ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS = ['adf', 'airflow'] as const;
export type OrchestrationProvider = (typeof ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS)[number];

export const PROVIDER_LABELS: Record<OrchestrationProvider, string> = {
  adf: 'Azure Data Factory',
  airflow: 'Apache Airflow',
};

/** Mirrors the backend `TriggerBindingRead`. */
export interface TriggerBinding {
  id: string;
  provider: OrchestrationProvider;
  pipeline_or_dag_id: string;
  env: string;
  suite_id: string;
  enabled: boolean;
}

/** Mirrors `TriggerBindingCreate`. */
export interface TriggerBindingCreate {
  provider: OrchestrationProvider;
  pipeline_or_dag_id: string;
  env: string;
  suite_id: string;
  enabled?: boolean;
}

export async function listTriggerBindings(suiteId: string): Promise<TriggerBinding[]> {
  const { data } = await api.get<TriggerBinding[]>('/trigger-bindings', {
    params: { suite_id: suiteId },
  });
  return data;
}

export async function createTriggerBinding(payload: TriggerBindingCreate): Promise<TriggerBinding> {
  const { data } = await api.post<TriggerBinding>('/trigger-bindings', payload);
  return data;
}

/** Toggle a binding on/off without deleting it (`PATCH` — the only mutable field). */
export async function setTriggerBindingEnabled(
  id: string,
  enabled: boolean,
): Promise<TriggerBinding> {
  const { data } = await api.patch<TriggerBinding>(`/trigger-bindings/${id}`, { enabled });
  return data;
}

export async function deleteTriggerBinding(id: string): Promise<void> {
  await api.delete(`/trigger-bindings/${id}`);
}

=== TriggersPanel.tsx ===
import { DeleteOutlined } from '@ant-design/icons';
import {
  App,
  Alert,
  Button,
  Card,
  Empty,
  Flex,
  Input,
  List,
  Select,
  Spin,
  Switch,
  Tag,
  Typography,
} from 'antd';
import { useState } from 'react';

import { CONNECTION_ENVS, type ConnectionEnv, ENV_COLORS, envLabel } from '../../api/connections';
import {
  createTriggerBinding,
  deleteTriggerBinding,
  listTriggerBindings,
  ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS,
  type OrchestrationProvider,
  PROVIDER_LABELS,
  setTriggerBindingEnabled,
  type TriggerBinding,
} from '../../api/triggerBindings';
import { useAsyncData } from '../../hooks/useAsyncData';

/**
 * Suite-detail panel for the suite's run triggers: bind an orchestrator pipeline/
 * DAG so the suite runs on that pipeline's *success* (CLAUDE.md §4 — orchestration
 * providers are never a datasource; this is the one place a pipeline id meets a
 * suite). Anyone with `view` sees the bindings; `edit`+ (`canManage`) gets the
 * add / enable-toggle / remove controls, matching the backend gate.
 */
export function TriggersPanel({ suiteId, canManage }: { suiteId: string; canManage: boolean }) {
  const { state, reload } = useAsyncData(() => listTriggerBindings(suiteId));

  return (
    <Card
      size="small"
      title={
        <Flex vertical gap={2}>
          <Typography.Text strong>Triggers</Typography.Text>
          <Typography.Text type="secondary" style={{ fontSize: 12, fontWeight: 400 }}>
            Run this suite when an orchestrator pipeline / DAG completes successfully.
          </Typography.Text>
        </Flex>
      }
    >
      <TriggersBody state={state} suiteId={suiteId} canManage={canManage} onChanged={reload} />
    </Card>
  );
}

function TriggersBody({
  state,
  suiteId,
  canManage,
  onChanged,
}: {
  state: ReturnType<typeof useAsyncData<TriggerBinding[]>>['state'];
  suiteId: string;
  canManage: boolean;
  onChanged: () => void;
}) {
  if (state.status === 'loading') {
    return <Spin tip="Loading triggers…" />;
  }
  if (state.status === 'error') {
    return (
      <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load triggers" description={state.error} />
    );
  }
  const bindings = state.data;

  return (
    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
      {canManage && <AddTrigger suiteId={suiteId} onAdded={onChanged} />}
      {bindings.length === 0 ? (
        <Empty
          image={Empty.PRESENTED_IMAGE_SIMPLE}
          description="No triggers — this suite runs only on manual / scheduled runs."
        />
      ) : (
        <List
          dataSource={bindings}
          renderItem={(binding) => (
            <TriggerRow
              key={binding.id}
              binding={binding}
              canManage={canManage}
              onChanged={onChanged}
            />
          )}
        />
      )}
    </Flex>
  );
}

function TriggerRow({
  binding,
  canManage,
  onChanged,
}: {
  binding: TriggerBinding;
  canManage: boolean;
  onChanged: () => void;
}) {
  const { message } = App.useApp();
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);

  const onToggle = async (enabled: boolean) => {
    setBusy(true);
    try {
      await setTriggerBindingEnabled(binding.id, enabled);
      message.success(`${binding.pipeline_or_dag_id}: ${enabled ? 'enabled' : 'disabled'}`);
      onChanged();
    } catch (err) {
      message.error(`Update failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
    } finally {
      setBusy(false);
    }
  };

  const onRemove = async () => {
    setBusy(true);
    try {
      await deleteTriggerBinding(binding.id);
      message.success(`${binding.pipeline_or_dag_id}: removed`);
      onChanged();
    } catch (err) {
      message.error(`Remove failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
    } finally {
      setBusy(false);
    }
  };

  return (
    <List.Item
      actions={
        canManage
          ? [
              <Switch
                key="toggle"
                size="small"
                checked={binding.enabled}
                loading={busy}
                onChange={onToggle}
                aria-label={`Enable ${binding.pipeline_or_dag_id}`}
              />,
              <Button
                key="remove"
                size="small"
                type="text"
                danger
                icon={<DeleteOutlined />}
                loading={busy}
                onClick={onRemove}
                aria-label={`Remove ${binding.pipeline_or_dag_id}`}
              />,
            ]
          : [<Tag key="state">{binding.enabled ? 'enabled' : 'disabled'}</Tag>]
      }
    >
      <Flex gap={10} align="center" style={{ minWidth: 0 }}>
        <Tag color={ENV_COLORS[binding.env as ConnectionEnv]}>
          {envLabel(binding.env as ConnectionEnv)}
        </Tag>
        <Flex vertical gap={2} style={{ minWidth: 0 }}>
          <Typography.Text code ellipsis>
            {binding.pipeline_or_dag_id}
          </Typography.Text>
          <Typography.Text type="secondary" style={{ fontSize: 12 }}>
            {PROVIDER_LABELS[binding.provider]}
          </Typography.Text>
        </Flex>
      </Flex>
    </List.Item>
  );
}

function AddTrigger({ suiteId, onAdded }: { suiteId: string; onAdded: () => void }) {
  const { message } = App.useApp();
  const [provider, setProvider] = useState<OrchestrationProvider>();
  const [env, setEnv] = useState<ConnectionEnv>();
  const [pipelineId, setPipelineId] = useState('');
  const [adding, setAdding] = useState(false);

  const onAdd = async () => {
    const id = pipelineId.trim();
    if (!provider || !env || !id) return;
    setAdding(true);
    try {
      await createTriggerBinding({ provider, env, pipeline_or_dag_id: id, suite_id: suiteId });
      message.success(`${id}: trigger added`);
      setProvider(undefined);
      setEnv(undefined);
      setPipelineId('');
      onAdded();
    } catch (err) {
      message.error(`Add failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
    } finally {
      setAdding(false);
    }
  };

  return (
    <Flex gap={8} align="center" wrap>
      <Select
        value={provider}
        onChange={setProvider}
        placeholder="Provider"
        style={{ width: 170 }}
        options={ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS.map((p) => ({ value: p, label: PROVIDER_LABELS[p] }))}
      />
      <Input
        value={pipelineId}
        onChange={(e) => setPipelineId(e.target.value)}
        placeholder="Pipeline / DAG id"
        style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 160 }}
        onPressEnter={onAdd}
      />
      <Select
        value={env}
        onChange={setEnv}
        placeholder="Env"
        style={{ width: 100 }}
        options={CONNECTION_ENVS.map((e) => ({ value: e, label: envLabel(e) }))}
      />
      <Button
        type="primary"
        loading={adding}
        disabled={!provider || !env || !pipelineId.trim()}
        onClick={onAdd}
      >
        Add
      </Button>
    </Flex>
  );
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/api/schedules.ts",
  "content": "import { api } from './client';\n\n/**\n * Schedules — cron-driven suite runs (A7). A schedule fires a run of its suite\n * on a 5-field cron cadence in a given IANA timezone; the backend precomputes\n * `next_run_at` and a Celery-beat tick dispatches due schedules. Managing a\n * schedule needs `edit` on the suite (backend-gated); listing needs `view`.\n * This is the only place a suite acquires an unattended run cadence — distinct\n * from trigger bindings (run-on-pipeline-success, CLAUDE.md §4).\n */\n\n/** Mirrors the backend `ScheduleRead`. */\nexport interface Schedule {\n  id: string;\n  suite_id: string;\n  cron: string;\n  timezone: string;\n  enabled: boolean;\n  next_run_at: string;\n  last_run_at: string | null;\n}\n\n/** Mirrors `ScheduleCreate`. */\nexport interface ScheduleCreate {\n  suite_id: string;\n  cron: string;\n  timezone?: string;\n  enabled?: boolean;\n}\n\n/** Mirrors `ScheduleUpdate` — partial; only supplied fields change. */\nexport interface ScheduleUpdate {\n  cron?: string;\n  timezone?: string;\n  enabled?: boolean;\n}\n\nexport async function listSchedules(suiteId: string): Promise<Schedule[]> {\n  const { data } = await api.get<Schedule[]>('/schedules', { params: { suite_id: suiteId } });\n  return data;\n}\n\nexport async function createSchedule(payload: ScheduleCreate): Promise<Schedule> {\n  const { data } = await api.post<Schedule>('/schedules', payload);\n  return data;\n}\n\nexport async function updateSchedule(id: string, payload: ScheduleUpdate): Promise<Schedule> {\n  const { data } = await api.patch<Schedule>(`/schedules/${id}`, payload);\n  return data;\n}\n\nexport async function deleteSchedule(id: string): Promise<void> {\n  await api.delete(`/schedules/${id}`);\n}\n\n/**\n * IANA timezones for the schedule editor. `Intl.supportedValuesOf` is the\n * runtime's own list (no hardcoded table to drift); falls back to UTC on the\n * rare engine that lacks it. UTC is hoisted to the front as the default.\n */\nexport function timezoneOptions(): string[] {\n  const supported =\n    typeof Intl.supportedValuesOf === 'function' ? Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone') : [];\n  const zones = supported.length > 0 ? supported : ['UTC'];\n  return ['UTC', ...zones.filter((z) => z !== 'UTC')];\n}\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/frontend/src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel.tsx",
  "content": "import { DeleteOutlined } from '@ant-design/icons';\nimport {\n  App,\n  Alert,\n  Button,\n  Card,\n  Empty,\n  Flex,\n  Input,\n  Select,\n  Spin,\n  Switch,\n  Table,\n  Tag,\n  Typography,\n} from 'antd';\nimport type { ColumnsType } from 'antd/es/table';\nimport { useMemo, useState } from 'react';\n\nimport {\n  createSchedule,\n  deleteSchedule,\n  type Schedule,\n  timezoneOptions,\n  updateSchedule,\n} from '../../api/schedules';\nimport { useAsyncData } from '../../hooks/useAsyncData';\nimport { formatTimestamp } from '../results/resultsFormat';\n\n/**\n * Suite-detail panel for cron-driven run schedules (A7). A schedule runs the\n * suite unattended on a 5-field cron cadence in an IANA timezone — distinct from\n * Triggers (run-on-pipeline-success). Anyone with `view` sees the schedules;\n * `edit`+ (`canManage`) gets the create / pause-toggle / delete controls, matching\n * the backend gate. Cron/timezone are validated server-side (422 → inline error).\n */\nexport function SchedulesPanel({ suiteId, canManage }: { suiteId: string; canManage: boolean }) {\n  const { state, reload } = useAsyncData(() => listSchedulesData(suiteId));\n\n  return (\n    <Card\n      size=\"small\"\n      title={\n        <Flex vertical gap={2}>\n          <Typography.Text strong>Schedules</Typography.Text>\n          <Typography.Text type=\"secondary\" style={{ fontSize: 12, fontWeight: 400 }}>\n            Run this suite automatically on a cron cadence.\n          </Typography.Text>\n        </Flex>\n      }\n    >\n      <SchedulesBody state={state} suiteId={suiteId} canManage={canManage} onChanged={reload} />\n    </Card>\n  );\n}\n\n// Indirection so the panel and tests share one fetch entry point (and the\n// component file owns no other side-effecting import surface).\nfunction listSchedulesData(suiteId: string) {\n  return import('../../api/schedules').then((m) => m.listSchedules(suiteId));\n}\n\nfunction SchedulesBody({\n  state,\n  suiteId,\n  canManage,\n  onChanged,\n}: {\n  state: ReturnType<typeof useAsyncData<Schedule[]>>['state'];\n  suiteId: string;\n  canManage: boolean;\n  onChanged: () => void;\n}) {\n  if (state.status === 'loading') {\n    return <Spin tip=\"Loading schedules…\" />;\n  }\n  if (state.status === 'error') {\n    return (\n      <Alert type=\"error\" showIcon message=\"Failed to load schedules\" description={state.error} />\n    );\n  }\n  const schedules = state.data;\n\n  return (\n    <Flex vertical gap={16}>\n      {canManage && <AddSchedule suiteId={suiteId} onAdded={onChanged} />}\n      {schedules.length === 0 ? (\n        <Empty\n          image={Empty.PRESENTED_IMAGE_SIMPLE}\n          description=\"No schedules — this suite runs only on manual / triggered runs.\"\n        />\n      ) : (\n        <ScheduleTable schedules={schedules} canManage={canManage} onChanged={onChanged} />\n      )}\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction ScheduleTable({\n  schedules,\n  canManage,\n  onChanged,\n}: {\n  schedules: Schedule[];\n  canManage: boolean;\n  onChanged: () => void;\n}) {\n  const { message } = App.useApp();\n  const [busyId, setBusyId] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  const onToggle = async (s: Schedule, enabled: boolean) => {\n    setBusyId(s.id);\n    try {\n      await updateSchedule(s.id, { enabled });\n      message.success(`${s.cron}: ${enabled ? 'resumed' : 'paused'}`);\n      onChanged();\n    } catch (err) {\n      message.error(`Update failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);\n    } finally {\n      setBusyId(null);\n    }\n  };\n\n  const onRemove = async (s: Schedule) => {\n    setBusyId(s.id);\n    try {\n      await deleteSchedule(s.id);\n      message.success(`${s.cron}: removed`);\n      onChanged();\n    } catch (err) {\n      message.error(`Remove failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);\n    } finally {\n      setBusyId(null);\n    }\n  };\n\n  const columns: ColumnsType<Schedule> = [\n    {\n      title: 'Cron',\n      dataIndex: 'cron',\n      render: (cron: string) => <Typography.Text code>{cron}</Typography.Text>,\n    },\n    { title: 'Timezone', dataIndex: 'timezone', width: 160 },\n    {\n      title: 'Next run',\n      dataIndex: 'next_run_at',\n      render: (t: string) => formatTimestamp(t),\n    },\n    {\n      title: 'Last run',\n      dataIndex: 'last_run_at',\n      render: (t: string | null) => formatTimestamp(t),\n    },\n    {\n      title: 'Status',\n      dataIndex: 'enabled',\n      width: 100,\n      render: (enabled: boolean, s) =>\n        canManage ? (\n          <Switch\n            size=\"small\"\n            checked={enabled}\n            loading={busyId === s.id}\n            onChange={(next) => onToggle(s, next)}\n            aria-label={`${enabled ? 'Pause' : 'Resume'} ${s.cron}`}\n          />\n        ) : (\n          <Tag color={enabled ? 'success' : 'default'}>{enabled ? 'enabled' : 'paused'}</Tag>\n        ),\n    },\n    ...(canManage\n      ? [\n          {\n            title: '',\n            key: 'actions',\n            width: 48,\n            render: (_: unknown, s: Schedule) => (\n              <Button\n                size=\"small\"\n                type=\"text\"\n                danger\n                icon={<DeleteOutlined />}\n                loading={busyId === s.id}\n                onClick={() => onRemove(s)}\n                aria-label={`Remove ${s.cron}`}\n              />\n            ),\n          },\n        ]\n      : []),\n  ];\n\n  return (\n    <Table<Schedule>\n      rowKey=\"id\"\n      size=\"small\"\n      columns={columns}\n      dataSource={schedules}\n      pagination={false}\n    />\n  );\n}\n\nfunction AddSchedule({ suiteId, onAdded }: { suiteId: string; onAdded: () => void }) {\n  const { message } = App.useApp();\n  const [cron, setCron] = useState('');\n  const [timezone, setTimezone] = useState('UTC');\n  const [adding, setAdding] = useState(false);\n  const zones = useMemo(() => timezoneOptions(), []);\n\n  const onAdd = async () => {\n    const expr = cron.trim();\n    if (!expr) return;\n    setAdding(true);\n    try {\n      await createSchedule({ suite_id: suiteId, cron: expr, timezone });\n      message.success(`${expr}: scheduled`);\n      setCron('');\n      onAdded();\n    } catch (err) {\n      message.error(`Add failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);\n    } finally {\n      setAdding(false);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <Flex gap={8} align=\"center\" wrap>\n      <Input\n        value={cron}\n        onChange={(e) => setCron(e.target.value)}\n        placeholder=\"Cron e.g. 0 9 * * 1-5 (min hour dom mon dow)\"\n        style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 220 }}\n        onPressEnter={onAdd}\n        aria-label=\"Cron expression\"\n      />\n      <Select\n        showSearch\n        value={timezone}\n        onChange={setTimezone}\n        style={{ width: 220 }}\n        options={zones.map((z) => ({ value: z, label: z }))}\n        aria-label=\"Timezone\"\n      />\n      <Button type=\"primary\" loading={adding} disabled={!cron.trim()} onClick={onAdd}>\n        Add\n      </Button>\n    </Flex>\n  );\n}\n"
}

  "content": "import { App as AntApp } from 'antd';\nimport { render, screen, waitFor } from '@testing-library/react';\nimport userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';\nimport { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';\n\nimport {\n  createSchedule,\n  deleteSchedule,\n  listSchedules,\n  type Schedule,\n  updateSchedule,\n} from '../../src/api/schedules';\nimport { SchedulesPanel } from '../../src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel';\n\nvi.mock('../../src/api/schedules', async (importOriginal) => {\n  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import('../../src/api/schedules')>();\n  return {\n    ...actual, // keep timezoneOptions real\n    listSchedules: vi.fn(),\n    createSchedule: vi.fn(),\n    updateSchedule: vi.fn(),\n    deleteSchedule: vi.fn(),\n  };\n});\n\nconst mockList = vi.mocked(listSchedules);\nconst mockCreate = vi.mocked(createSchedule);\nconst mockUpdate = vi.mocked(updateSchedule);\nconst mockDelete = vi.mocked(deleteSchedule);\n\nconst SCHEDULE: Schedule = {\n  id: 'sch1',\n  suite_id: 's1',\n  cron: '0 9 * * 1-5',\n  timezone: 'UTC',\n  enabled: true,\n  next_run_at: '2026-06-21T09:00:00Z',\n  last_run_at: null,\n};\n\nfunction renderPanel(props: Partial<Parameters<typeof SchedulesPanel>[0]> = {}) {\n  return render(\n    <AntApp>\n      <SchedulesPanel suiteId=\"s1\" canManage {...props} />\n    </AntApp>,\n  );\n}\n\nafterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks());\n\ndescribe('SchedulesPanel', () => {\n  it('lists schedules with cron and a pause control', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);\n    renderPanel();\n\n    expect(await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5')).toBeInTheDocument();\n    // An enabled schedule offers a Pause toggle.\n    expect(screen.getByRole('switch', { name: 'Pause 0 9 * * 1-5' })).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('shows an empty state when there are no schedules', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([]);\n    renderPanel();\n\n    expect(\n      await screen.findByText('No schedules — this suite runs only on manual / triggered runs.'),\n    ).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n\n  it('creates a schedule from the cron + timezone form (UTC default)', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([]);\n    mockCreate.mockResolvedValue(SCHEDULE);\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    renderPanel();\n    await screen.findByText(/No schedules/);\n\n    await user.type(screen.getByLabelText('Cron expression'), '0 9 * * *');\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add' }));\n\n    await waitFor(() =>\n      expect(mockCreate).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        suite_id: 's1',\n        cron: '0 9 * * *',\n        timezone: 'UTC',\n      }),\n    );\n  });\n\n  it('pauses a schedule via the toggle', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);\n    mockUpdate.mockResolvedValue({ ...SCHEDULE, enabled: false });\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    renderPanel();\n    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('switch', { name: 'Pause 0 9 * * 1-5' }));\n\n    await waitFor(() => expect(mockUpdate).toHaveBeenCalledWith('sch1', { enabled: false }));\n  });\n\n  it('deletes a schedule', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);\n    mockDelete.mockResolvedValue();\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    renderPanel();\n    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');\n\n    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Remove 0 9 * * 1-5' }));\n\n    await waitFor(() => expect(mockDelete).toHaveBeenCalledWith('sch1'));\n  });\n\n  it('is read-only for a non-editor (no add form, no toggle/remove)', async () => {\n    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);\n    renderPanel({ canManage: false });\n    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');\n\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Add' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(screen.queryByRole('switch')).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n    expect(\n      screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Remove 0 9 * * 1-5' }),\n    ).not.toBeInTheDocument();\n    // The enabled state is shown read-only as a tag.\n    expect(screen.getByText('enabled')).toBeInTheDocument();\n  });\n});\n"
}

223	### Execution UI (5 tasks — 2/5 ✅)
224	- [ ] ⬜ Run now panel — suite picker, env / datasource, notification target
225	- [x] ✅ Live run progress UI — check-by-check status with spinner + cancel button — `LiveRunProgress` drawer (`components/runs/`): opens on the run id returned by the manual **Run** button (#245) instead of bouncing to `/results`, and polls `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (#301) on a self-scheduling timeout (not `setInterval` — no overlapping fetches) until the run is terminal (`succeeded`/`failed`/`cancelled`), then stops. Shows the run-status tag + a completed/total `Progress` bar + a per-check list (a `Spin`+"pending" while a check's `status` is null, its severity `Tag` once resolved). An editor (`canRun`) gets a **Cancel** button (`POST /runs/{id}/cancel` #302) on a non-terminal run; cancel reflects the terminal state immediately + the next poll confirms it. On terminal, a "View full results →" link to `/results`. Body keyed by run id so opening a new run restarts polling; `pollMs` is injectable for deterministic tests. 7 component tests (pending/resolved render, poll-until-terminal-then-stop, cancel happy path, cancel hidden when terminal / non-editor, fetch-error alert). **(A3)**
226	- [ ] ⬜ Scheduled runs table — create, pause, delete cron schedules

diff --git a/frontend/src/api/schedules.ts b/frontend/src/api/schedules.ts
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..21bbd5b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/frontend/src/api/schedules.ts
@@ -0,0 +1,67 @@
+import { api } from './client';
+
+/**
+ * Schedules — cron-driven suite runs (A7). A schedule fires a run of its suite
+ * on a 5-field cron cadence in a given IANA timezone; the backend precomputes
+ * `next_run_at` and a Celery-beat tick dispatches due schedules. Managing a
+ * schedule needs `edit` on the suite (backend-gated); listing needs `view`.
+ * This is the only place a suite acquires an unattended run cadence — distinct
+ * from trigger bindings (run-on-pipeline-success, CLAUDE.md §4).
+ */
+
+/** Mirrors the backend `ScheduleRead`. */
+export interface Schedule {
+  id: string;
+  suite_id: string;
+  cron: string;
+  timezone: string;
+  enabled: boolean;
+  next_run_at: string;
+  last_run_at: string | null;
+}
+
+/** Mirrors `ScheduleCreate`. */
+export interface ScheduleCreate {
+  suite_id: string;
+  cron: string;
+  timezone?: string;
+  enabled?: boolean;
+}
+
+/** Mirrors `ScheduleUpdate` — partial; only supplied fields change. */
+export interface ScheduleUpdate {
+  cron?: string;
+  timezone?: string;
+  enabled?: boolean;
+}
+
+export async function listSchedules(suiteId: string): Promise<Schedule[]> {
+  const { data } = await api.get<Schedule[]>('/schedules', { params: { suite_id: suiteId } });
+  return data;
+}
+
+export async function createSchedule(payload: ScheduleCreate): Promise<Schedule> {
+  const { data } = await api.post<Schedule>('/schedules', payload);
+  return data;
+}
+
+export async function updateSchedule(id: string, payload: ScheduleUpdate): Promise<Schedule> {
+  const { data } = await api.patch<Schedule>(`/schedules/${id}`, payload);
+  return data;
+}
+
+export async function deleteSchedule(id: string): Promise<void> {
+  await api.delete(`/schedules/${id}`);
+}
+
+/**
+ * IANA timezones for the schedule editor. `Intl.supportedValuesOf` is the
+ * runtime's own list (no hardcoded table to drift); falls back to UTC on the
+ * rare engine that lacks it. UTC is hoisted to the front as the default.
+ */
+export function timezoneOptions(): string[] {
+  const supported =
+    typeof Intl.supportedValuesOf === 'function' ? Intl.supportedValuesOf('timeZone') : [];
+  const zones = supported.length > 0 ? supported : ['UTC'];
+  return ['UTC', ...zones.filter((z) => z !== 'UTC')];
+}
diff --git a/frontend/src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel.tsx b/frontend/src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel.tsx
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..11439dd
--- /dev/null
+++ b/frontend/src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel.tsx
@@ -0,0 +1,245 @@
+import { DeleteOutlined } from '@ant-design/icons';
+import {
+  App,
+  Alert,
+  Button,
+  Card,
+  Empty,
+  Flex,
+  Input,
+  Select,
+  Spin,
+  Switch,
+  Table,
+  Tag,
+  Typography,
+} from 'antd';
+import type { ColumnsType } from 'antd/es/table';
+import { useMemo, useState } from 'react';
+
+import {
+  createSchedule,
+  deleteSchedule,
+  listSchedules,
+  type Schedule,
+  timezoneOptions,
+  updateSchedule,
+} from '../../api/schedules';
+import { useAsyncData } from '../../hooks/useAsyncData';
+import { formatTimestamp } from '../results/resultsFormat';
+
+/**
+ * Suite-detail panel for cron-driven run schedules (A7). A schedule runs the
+ * suite unattended on a 5-field cron cadence in an IANA timezone — distinct from
+ * Triggers (run-on-pipeline-success). Anyone with `view` sees the schedules;
+ * `edit`+ (`canManage`) gets the create / pause-toggle / delete controls, matching
+ * the backend gate. Cron/timezone are validated server-side (422 → inline error).
+ */
+export function SchedulesPanel({ suiteId, canManage }: { suiteId: string; canManage: boolean }) {
+  const { state, reload } = useAsyncData(() => listSchedules(suiteId));
+
+  return (
+    <Card
+      size="small"
+      title={
+        <Flex vertical gap={2}>
+          <Typography.Text strong>Schedules</Typography.Text>
+          <Typography.Text type="secondary" style={{ fontSize: 12, fontWeight: 400 }}>
+            Run this suite automatically on a cron cadence.
+          </Typography.Text>
+        </Flex>
+      }
+    >
+      <SchedulesBody state={state} suiteId={suiteId} canManage={canManage} onChanged={reload} />
+    </Card>
+  );
+}
+
+function SchedulesBody({
+  state,
+  suiteId,
+  canManage,
+  onChanged,
+}: {
+  state: ReturnType<typeof useAsyncData<Schedule[]>>['state'];
+  suiteId: string;
+  canManage: boolean;
+  onChanged: () => void;
+}) {
+  if (state.status === 'loading') {
+    return <Spin tip="Loading schedules…" />;
+  }
+  if (state.status === 'error') {
+    return (
+      <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load schedules" description={state.error} />
+    );
+  }
+  const schedules = state.data;
+
+  return (
+    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
+      {canManage && <AddSchedule suiteId={suiteId} onAdded={onChanged} />}
+      {schedules.length === 0 ? (
+        <Empty
+          image={Empty.PRESENTED_IMAGE_SIMPLE}
+          description="No schedules — this suite runs only on manual / triggered runs."
+        />
+      ) : (
+        <ScheduleTable schedules={schedules} canManage={canManage} onChanged={onChanged} />
+      )}
+    </Flex>
+  );
+}
+
+function ScheduleTable({
+  schedules,
+  canManage,
+  onChanged,
+}: {
+  schedules: Schedule[];
+  canManage: boolean;
+  onChanged: () => void;
+}) {
+  const { message } = App.useApp();
+  const [busyId, setBusyId] = useState<string | null>(null);
+
+  const onToggle = async (s: Schedule, enabled: boolean) => {
+    setBusyId(s.id);
+    try {
+      await updateSchedule(s.id, { enabled });
+      message.success(`${s.cron}: ${enabled ? 'resumed' : 'paused'}`);
+      onChanged();
+    } catch (err) {
+      message.error(`Update failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
+    } finally {
+      setBusyId(null);
+    }
+  };
+
+  const onRemove = async (s: Schedule) => {
+    setBusyId(s.id);
+    try {
+      await deleteSchedule(s.id);
+      message.success(`${s.cron}: removed`);
+      onChanged();
+    } catch (err) {
+      message.error(`Remove failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
+    } finally {
+      setBusyId(null);
+    }
+  };
+
+  const columns: ColumnsType<Schedule> = [
+    {
+      title: 'Cron',
+      dataIndex: 'cron',
+      render: (cron: string) => <Typography.Text code>{cron}</Typography.Text>,
+    },
+    { title: 'Timezone', dataIndex: 'timezone', width: 160 },
+    {
+      title: 'Next run',
+      dataIndex: 'next_run_at',
+      render: (t: string) => formatTimestamp(t),
+    },
+    {
+      title: 'Last run',
+      dataIndex: 'last_run_at',
+      render: (t: string | null) => formatTimestamp(t),
+    },
+    {
+      title: 'Status',
+      dataIndex: 'enabled',
+      width: 100,
+      render: (enabled: boolean, s) =>
+        canManage ? (
+          <Switch
+            size="small"
+            checked={enabled}
+            loading={busyId === s.id}
+            onChange={(next) => onToggle(s, next)}
+            aria-label={`${enabled ? 'Pause' : 'Resume'} ${s.cron}`}
+          />
+        ) : (
+          <Tag color={enabled ? 'success' : 'default'}>{enabled ? 'enabled' : 'paused'}</Tag>
+        ),
+    },
+    ...(canManage
+      ? [
+          {
+            title: '',
+            key: 'actions',
+            width: 48,
+            render: (_: unknown, s: Schedule) => (
+              <Button
+                size="small"
+                type="text"
+                danger
+                icon={<DeleteOutlined />}
+                loading={busyId === s.id}
+                onClick={() => onRemove(s)}
+                aria-label={`Remove ${s.cron}`}
+              />
+            ),
+          },
+        ]
+      : []),
+  ];
+
+  return (
+    <Table<Schedule>
+      rowKey="id"
+      size="small"
+      columns={columns}
+      dataSource={schedules}
+      pagination={false}
+    />
+  );
+}
+
+function AddSchedule({ suiteId, onAdded }: { suiteId: string; onAdded: () => void }) {
+  const { message } = App.useApp();
+  const [cron, setCron] = useState('');
+  const [timezone, setTimezone] = useState('UTC');
+  const [adding, setAdding] = useState(false);
+  const zones = useMemo(() => timezoneOptions(), []);
+
+  const onAdd = async () => {
+    const expr = cron.trim();
+    if (!expr) return;
+    setAdding(true);
+    try {
+      await createSchedule({ suite_id: suiteId, cron: expr, timezone });
+      message.success(`${expr}: scheduled`);
+      setCron('');
+      onAdded();
+    } catch (err) {
+      message.error(`Add failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
+    } finally {
+      setAdding(false);
+    }
+  };
+
+  return (
+    <Flex gap={8} align="center" wrap>
+      <Input
+        value={cron}
+        onChange={(e) => setCron(e.target.value)}
+        placeholder="Cron e.g. 0 9 * * 1-5 (min hour dom mon dow)"
+        style={{ flex: 1, minWidth: 220 }}
+        onPressEnter={onAdd}
+        aria-label="Cron expression"
+      />
+      <Select
+        showSearch
+        value={timezone}
+        onChange={setTimezone}
+        style={{ width: 220 }}
+        options={zones.map((z) => ({ value: z, label: z }))}
+        aria-label="Timezone"
+      />
+      <Button type="primary" loading={adding} disabled={!cron.trim()} onClick={onAdd}>
+        Add
+      </Button>
+    </Flex>
+  );
+}

diff --git a/frontend/tests/components/SchedulesPanel.test.tsx b/frontend/tests/components/SchedulesPanel.test.tsx
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..5e355b9
--- /dev/null
+++ b/frontend/tests/components/SchedulesPanel.test.tsx
@@ -0,0 +1,124 @@
+import { App as AntApp } from 'antd';
+import { render, screen, waitFor } from '@testing-library/react';
+import userEvent from '@testing-library/user-event';
+import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
+
+import {
+  createSchedule,
+  deleteSchedule,
+  listSchedules,
+  type Schedule,
+  updateSchedule,
+} from '../../src/api/schedules';
+import { SchedulesPanel } from '../../src/components/suites/SchedulesPanel';
+
+vi.mock('../../src/api/schedules', async (importOriginal) => {
+  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import('../../src/api/schedules')>();
+  return {
+    ...actual, // keep timezoneOptions real
+    listSchedules: vi.fn(),
+    createSchedule: vi.fn(),
+    updateSchedule: vi.fn(),
+    deleteSchedule: vi.fn(),
+  };
+});
+
+const mockList = vi.mocked(listSchedules);
+const mockCreate = vi.mocked(createSchedule);
+const mockUpdate = vi.mocked(updateSchedule);
+const mockDelete = vi.mocked(deleteSchedule);
+
+const SCHEDULE: Schedule = {
+  id: 'sch1',
+  suite_id: 's1',
+  cron: '0 9 * * 1-5',
+  timezone: 'UTC',
+  enabled: true,
+  next_run_at: '2026-06-21T09:00:00Z',
+  last_run_at: null,
+};
+
+function renderPanel(props: Partial<Parameters<typeof SchedulesPanel>[0]> = {}) {
+  return render(
+    <AntApp>
+      <SchedulesPanel suiteId="s1" canManage {...props} />
+    </AntApp>,
+  );
+}
+
+afterEach(() => vi.clearAllMocks());
+
+describe('SchedulesPanel', () => {
+  it('lists schedules with cron and a pause control', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);
+    renderPanel();
+
+    expect(await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5')).toBeInTheDocument();
+    // An enabled schedule offers a Pause toggle.
+    expect(screen.getByRole('switch', { name: 'Pause 0 9 * * 1-5' })).toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('shows an empty state when there are no schedules', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([]);
+    renderPanel();
+
+    expect(
+      await screen.findByText('No schedules — this suite runs only on manual / triggered runs.'),
+    ).toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+
+  it('creates a schedule from the cron + timezone form (UTC default)', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([]);
+    mockCreate.mockResolvedValue(SCHEDULE);
+    const user = userEvent.setup();
+    renderPanel();
+    await screen.findByText(/No schedules/);
+
+    await user.type(screen.getByLabelText('Cron expression'), '0 9 * * *');
+    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Add' }));
+
+    await waitFor(() =>
+      expect(mockCreate).toHaveBeenCalledWith({
+        suite_id: 's1',
+        cron: '0 9 * * *',
+        timezone: 'UTC',
+      }),
+    );
+  });
+
+  it('pauses a schedule via the toggle', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);
+    mockUpdate.mockResolvedValue({ ...SCHEDULE, enabled: false });
+    const user = userEvent.setup();
+    renderPanel();
+    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');
+
+    await user.click(screen.getByRole('switch', { name: 'Pause 0 9 * * 1-5' }));
+
+    await waitFor(() => expect(mockUpdate).toHaveBeenCalledWith('sch1', { enabled: false }));
+  });
+
+  it('deletes a schedule', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);
+    mockDelete.mockResolvedValue();
+    const user = userEvent.setup();
+    renderPanel();
+    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');
+
+    await user.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Remove 0 9 * * 1-5' }));
+
+    await waitFor(() => expect(mockDelete).toHaveBeenCalledWith('sch1'));
+  });
+
+  it('is read-only for a non-editor (no add form, no toggle/remove)', async () => {
+    mockList.mockResolvedValue([SCHEDULE]);
+    renderPanel({ canManage: false });
+    await screen.findByText('0 9 * * 1-5');
+
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Add' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('switch')).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+    expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'Remove 0 9 * * 1-5' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
+    // The enabled state is shown read-only as a tag.
+    expect(screen.getByText('enabled')).toBeInTheDocument();
+  });
+});
```

429:class Schedule(Base):
430:    """A cron schedule that fires a suite run automatically (A7).
433:    minute and queries `enabled AND next_run_at <= now()` — an indexed scan, so

429	class Schedule(Base):
430	    """A cron schedule that fires a suite run automatically (A7).
431	
432	    The beat dispatcher (`worker.tasks.dispatch_due_schedules`) ticks every
433	    minute and queries `enabled AND next_run_at <= now()` — an indexed scan, so
434	    the cron is parsed only when a schedule actually fires, never per tick.
435	
436	    `next_run_at` is precomputed (`services.cron.next_fire`) on create and after
437	    each fire / cron change. **No-backfill semantics**: a fire advances it to the
438	    next *future* occurrence, so a downtime gap fires at most once on recovery
439	    rather than backfilling every missed slot (correct for monitoring). `cron` is
440	    a standard 5-field expression evaluated in `timezone` (IANA, DST-aware,
441	    default UTC). Cascade-deleted with the suite.
442	    """
443	
444	    __tablename__ = "schedules"
445	    __table_args__ = (
446	        Index("ix_schedules_suite_id", "suite_id"),
447	        # The dispatcher's hot path: due + enabled schedules, oldest-due first.
448	        Index("ix_schedules_enabled_next_run_at", "enabled", "next_run_at"),
449	    )

I have enough to finalize. The test confirms the delete test (line 108) deletes immediately with no confirmation step asserted — consistent with the missing-confirm finding. The aria-label uses cron only, so two schedules sharing a cron with different timezones collide. Let me verify the `timezoneOptions` edge once more — the function looks safe (UTC always hoisted, filter removes other UTC), but the fallback `['UTC']` then filtered yields `['UTC']` — fine, no empty list, no dup.

Let me double check one timing concern: `busyId` set during an op, then `onChanged()` triggers reload (async), then `finally` sets `busyId=null`. The reload replaces the data; if the deleted row is gone, busyId is moot. For toggle, the row remains. No stale-busyId persists because `finally` always clears it synchronously after the await. The real concern is concurrent ops on two different rows: clicking row A's toggle, then row B's delete while A is in-flight — `setBusyId(B.id)` overwrites, so A's spinner disappears mid-flight and B's shows; then whichever `finally` runs last sets null. Cosmetic, low severity but real.

Here are my findings.

If the backend ever returns next_run_at: null for a paused schedule (the service recomputes next_run_at on re-enable, implying it may be cleared/stale while paused), the TS type is wrong. formatTimestamp(t) defends at runtime (returns em dash for null), so no crash — but the interface/column-render type assertion masks the real nullability and a future caller doing schedule.next_run_at.slice(...) would NPE. Last-run column at line 147 correctly types string | null; next-run should match."
  }
]
```

Summary: 5 concrete findings. The two highest-value are the missing delete confirmation (line 119 — diverges from the codebase's own modal.confirm convention for destructive actions) and the cron-only aria-labels (line 160 — duplicate labels break both getByRole and screen-reader disambiguation when two schedules share a cron across timezones). The shared `busyId` (line 104) is a real regression from TriggersPanel's per-row pattern but cosmetic. The 422-message gap (schedules.ts) only affects Pydantic Field-constraint failures since semantic cron/tz errors are properly typed `DataQError`s. The `next_run_at` nullability is a type-honesty issue with runtime defense already in place. `timezoneOptions()` was checked and is sound — no duplicate UTC, no empty list.

```
Dedupe the create-queued-run + dispatch + broker-failure pattern across trigger paths
---
## Context
Three sites create a queued `Run`, hand it to `run_dispatch.dispatch_run`, and on broker failure mark it terminal-`failed`:
- `api/v1/probe.py` `trigger_snowflake_probe`
- `api/v1/suites.py` `trigger_suite_run` (manual run, PR-C0b #225)
- `services/orchestration_service.py` `_trigger_suites` (pipeline-success, batch)

The broker-failure DB shape had **already diverged** once: `_trigger_suites` set `status=failed` **and** `finished_at=now()`, while `probe` and the new manual path set only `status`. PR-C0b aligned the manual path (finished_at set), but `probe.py` still leaves `finished_at` NULL, and the create+dispatch+503 block is duplicated.

## Ask
Extract a shared helper (e.g. `run_dispatch.create_and_dispatch_run(session, *, suite_id, triggered_by) -> Run`, or a `mark_dispatch_failed(run)` for the failure shape) so the terminal-failed contract is one decision. Note: `_trigger_suites` dispatches a **batch** in a loop, so the helper must fit that shape or split into create vs failure-shape pieces.

## Acceptance
- One definition of the dispatch-failure run shape; `probe.py` aligned (finished_at set).
- No behaviour change to happy paths; existing tests stay green.

_Surfaced by the PR-C0b multi-agent review (altitude/reuse)._

=== run_dispatch.py ===
"""Dispatch a persisted `Run` to the Celery worker — the one place that publishes.

Publishes ``run_suite`` **by name** via ``celery_app.send_task`` rather than
importing the task object. That decoupling is deliberate: ``worker.tasks``
imports service modules (e.g. ``orchestration_service``), so a service importing
``worker.tasks`` back would be a cyclic import (CodeQL `py/cyclic-import`). By
name there is no import edge service → worker, and the broker resolves the task
on the worker side. The ``before_task_publish`` signal still fires, so the
``request_id`` correlation header is carried exactly as for ``.delay``.

Raises on a broker/publish failure; the caller owns the policy for a stuck run
(the probe endpoint surfaces 503; the pipeline-trigger path marks the run
``failed`` so it isn't left ``queued``).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
from backend.app.db.models import Run
from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app

log = get_logger(__name__)

_RUN_SUITE_TASK = "run_suite"


def dispatch_run(run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
    """Publish the ``run_suite`` task for ``run_id`` and return its Celery task id.

    The task id is stored on the `Run` (``celery_task_id``) so a later cancel can
    revoke a still-queued task. Raises if the broker is down — the caller owns the
    policy for the stuck run (`mark_dispatch_failed` + 503 / log).

    No 2-phase commit spans the broker and the DB: if the publish succeeds but the
    caller's follow-up commit of ``celery_task_id`` fails (a rare DB blip in that
    window), the task still runs — the worker just can't be revoked by id and falls
    back to the cooperative ``cancelled``-status check. Self-correcting and benign.
    """
    result = celery_app.send_task(_RUN_SUITE_TASK, args=[str(run_id)])
    return str(result.id)


def mark_dispatch_failed(run: Run) -> None:
    """The canonical terminal-failed shape for a broker/dispatch failure.

    One definition shared by every trigger path (probe, manual run, pipeline
    success) so a never-dispatched run is recorded identically everywhere:
    ``failed`` with ``finished_at`` set and ``started_at`` left as-is (NULL — it
    never started), keeping run-history / duration views consistent (#227).
    """
    run.status = "failed"
    run.finished_at = datetime.now(UTC)


def revoke_run(task_id: str | None) -> None:
    """Best-effort revoke of a dispatched run's Celery task.

    Drops the task if it's still **queued** (not yet picked up). Deliberately no
    ``terminate`` — we don't SIGKILL a worker mid-GX (it would take out sibling
    tasks); an already-running task is stopped **cooperatively** (the worker
    checks for a ``cancelled`` run status). A no-op for an un-dispatched run
    (``task_id is None``); broker errors are swallowed (the DB status is already
    ``cancelled`` and the worker's cooperative check still applies).
    """
    if not task_id:
        return
    try:
        celery_app.control.revoke(task_id)
    except Exception:
        log.warning("run_revoke_failed", celery_task_id=task_id)
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "def revoke_run(task_id: str | None) -> None:",
  "new_string": "def dispatch_or_fail(session: Session, run: Run) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Dispatch a committed queued ``run``; on broker failure record the canonical\n    terminal-failed shape. Returns ``True`` if dispatched, ``False`` if the broker\n    was unreachable (the run is now ``failed`` with ``finished_at`` set, committed).\n\n    The one copy of the dispatch + broker-failure block every trigger path shares\n    (probe, manual run, pipeline-success batch, scheduled run) so a never-dispatched\n    run is recorded — and its traceback logged — identically everywhere (#227). The\n    caller owns the *policy* for a ``False`` return: the HTTP paths surface 503; the\n    batch / scheduled paths skip the run and carry on. Mirrors ``run_suite``'s own\n    no-2-phase-commit contract (see ``dispatch_run``): the publish and the\n    ``celery_task_id`` commit aren't atomic, which is benign and self-correcting.\n    \"\"\"\n    try:\n        run.celery_task_id = dispatch_run(run.id)\n        session.commit()\n        return True\n    except Exception:\n        log.exception(\"run_dispatch_failed\", run_id=str(run.id))\n        mark_dispatch_failed(run)\n        session.commit()\n        return False\n\n\ndef revoke_run(task_id: str | None) -> None:"
}
```

1	"""Unit tests for run_dispatch — dispatch / revoke / dispatch-failure shape.
2	
3	No broker: celery_app.send_task is spied. Asserts the task is published by its
4	registered name with the run id as the sole arg, that the captured task id is
5	returned, that a publish failure propagates (callers own the stuck-run policy),
6	and the canonical terminal-failed shape (#227).
7	"""
8	
9	import uuid
10	from types import SimpleNamespace
11	from typing import Any
12	
13	import pytest
14	
15	from backend.app.db.models import Run
16	from backend.app.services import run_dispatch
17	
18	pytestmark = pytest.mark.real_dispatch
19	
20	
21	def test_dispatch_run_sends_task_by_name_and_returns_task_id(
22	    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,
23	) -> None:
24	    calls: list[tuple[str, list[str]]] = []
25	
26	    def _send(name: str, args: list[str]) -> SimpleNamespace:
27	        calls.append((name, args))
28	        return SimpleNamespace(id="celery-task-123")
29	
30	    monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch.celery_app, "send_task", _send)
31	    run_id = uuid.uuid4()
32	
33	    task_id = run_dispatch.dispatch_run(run_id)
34	
35	    # Published by registered name (decoupled from worker.tasks — no import edge),
36	    # with the run id stringified for JSON serialisation; the AsyncResult id is
37	    # returned so the caller can store it on the run for later revoke.
38	    assert calls == [("run_suite", [str(run_id)])]
39	    assert task_id == "celery-task-123"
40	
41	
42	def test_mark_dispatch_failed_sets_canonical_shape() -> None:
43	    """#227: one definition of the dispatch-failure shape — failed + finished_at
44	    set, started_at left NULL (it never started)."""
45	    run = Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=uuid.uuid4(), status="queued")
46	    run_dispatch.mark_dispatch_failed(run)
47	    assert run.status == "failed"
48	    assert run.finished_at is not None
49	    assert run.started_at is None
50	
51	
52	def test_revoke_run_noop_without_task_id(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
53	    called = False
54	
55	    def _revoke(_task_id: str) -> None:
56	        nonlocal called
57	        called = True
58	
59	    monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch.celery_app.control, "revoke", _revoke)
60	    run_dispatch.revoke_run(None)  # un-dispatched run → no broker call
61	    assert called is False
62	
63	
64	def test_revoke_run_swallows_broker_error(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
65	    def _boom(_task_id: str) -> None:
66	        raise RuntimeError("control bus unreachable")
67	
68	    monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch.celery_app.control, "revoke", _boom)
69	    # Best-effort: the DB status is already 'cancelled' + the worker checks
70	    # cooperatively, so a broker error must not propagate.
71	    run_dispatch.revoke_run("task-1")
72	
73	
74	def test_dispatch_run_propagates_broker_failure(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
75	    def _boom(name: str, args: Any) -> None:
76	        raise RuntimeError("broker unreachable")
77	
78	    monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch.celery_app, "send_task", _boom)
79	
80	    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
81	        run_dispatch.dispatch_run(uuid.uuid4())

27:| **Next milestone** | **Week 5 in progress (16/19)** — async-execution backend: `error` status (#297), flat-file batch + `skip` (#298), UC run-path coverage (#299), run progress API (#301), cancel run + #227 dedup (#302), `GET /orchestration/pipelines` (#305), gap-recovery beat (#307), scheduling backend (A7) all shipped on top of the early-credit (Celery runner PR 4, worker dispatch #146, ADF/Airflow polling #171, `trigger_bindings` #172, target+dispatch #215, manual run + read API PR-C0b, Suite Triggers UI #216); plus the **live run-progress UI (A3)** and **scheduled-runs UI (A6)** on the frontend. **Remaining:** execution UI (run-now / recent-runs), run-history retention purge. |
207:- [x] ✅ Cancel run endpoint — gracefully terminate in-progress Celery task — `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` (edit-gated, suite-scoped, 409 if terminal): marks `cancelled` + best-effort `revoke` of the captured `runs.celery_task_id` (migration `d5e6f7a8b9c0`). Cooperative — no SIGKILL mid-GX: worker skips a cancelled run at pickup + `execute_run` won't overwrite a mid-run cancel. Deterministic for queued, best-effort for in-flight. Folded **#227** (one dispatch-failure shape in `run_dispatch`, aligning probe's `finished_at`). **[PR #302](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/302)** (A2)
211:- [x] ✅ **Scheduling backend** — cron-driven suite runs make "scheduling operational" (the backend half of the Execution-UI scheduled-runs table). New `schedules` table (migration `f7a8b9c0d1e2`, additive; cascade-deleted with the suite) holding a 5-field `cron` + IANA `timezone` (DST-aware) + precomputed `next_run_at` (indexed) per suite. `services/cron.py` wraps `croniter` + `zoneinfo` (validate cron/tz → 422; **no-backfill** `next_fire` = next fire strictly after the base, so a downtime gap fires once, not per missed slot). `schedule_service` CRUD mirrors `trigger_binding_service` (suite-permission-gated: `edit` to mutate, `view` to read; `next_run_at` recomputed on cadence-change / re-enable). `api/v1/schedules.py` (POST/GET/GET{id}/PATCH/DELETE). New Celery-beat tick `dispatch_due_schedules` (60s) scans `enabled, next_run_at<=now` with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (no double-fire across overlapping ticks), resolves the suite target up front (invalid → skip + advance, never a queued→failed run), queues a `Run` (`triggered_by=schedule:<id>`) + dispatches via the shared `run_dispatch` (broker-fail → `mark_dispatch_failed` #227). `cron.validate_cron` probes a fire so an impossible-calendar cron (Feb 30 — `croniter.is_valid` passes it) is a clean 422, not a 500-on-create / crashed dispatcher tick; the dispatcher self-disables such a row instead of hot-looping. 37 tests (cron units incl. DST spring-forward/fall-back + no-backfill + impossible-date; DB-backed CRUD/authz/422 API; dispatcher due/future/disabled/invalid-target/invalid-cron/no-backfill/broker-fail). **(A7)**
230:**Week 5 total: 16 / 19** _(early credit: Celery task runner in PR 4; worker runner-dispatch #146; ADF + Airflow 10-min polling #171; plus `trigger_bindings` CRUD #172 out-of-band; check target-table + dispatch ungate #215 (PR-C0a); manual run trigger + run/result read API (PR-C0b); **Suite Triggers UI #216 (PR #247)**. **This stretch (Phase 1–4):** `error` status #297, flat-file batch + `skip` #298, UC run-path coverage #299, run progress API #301, cancel run + #227 #302, `GET /orchestration/pipelines` #305, gap-recovery beat #307, **scheduling backend (A7)**, **live run-progress UI (A3)**, **scheduled-runs UI (A6)**. +3 tasks re-tracked here: check target-table #215 (slipped from W3), Suite Triggers UI #216, and the run-enablement read API (PR-C0b); +1 added: the scheduling backend (A7), the backend the roadmap's scheduled-runs UI task implies. The **manual Run button** (PR #245) + the **run-target editor** (PR #246) also shipped earlier this week. **Remaining (3):** run-history retention, run-now panel, recent-runs audit table)_

The failure *shape*\nwas already unified in #302 (mark_dispatch_failed); this folds the\nsurrounding try/dispatch/except block into one helper too.\n\nrun_dispatch.dispatch_or_fail(session, run) -> bool dispatches, sets\ncelery_task_id, commits; on broker failure it logs the traceback,\nmarks the run terminal-failed, commits, and returns False. Callers own\nthe policy for False: the HTTP paths raise 503, the batch/scheduled\npaths skip and carry on. Net effect: one definition of the dispatch +\nbroker-failure behaviour, and the probe/manual paths now log the broker\ntraceback they previously swallowed.\n\nNo happy-path behaviour change. Full backend suite green (804); added\ntwo unit tests for dispatch_or_fail (success + broker-failure).\n\nFixes #227\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF",

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ\ngit push -u origin fix/issue-227-dispatch-dedup 2>&1 | tail -3\ngh pr create --base main --head fix/issue-227-dispatch-dedup --title \"refactor: extract run_dispatch.dispatch_or_fail across trigger paths (#227)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What\n\nDedupes the create-adjacent **dispatch + broker-failure** block that was copy-pasted across all four trigger paths:\n- `api/v1/probe.py` `trigger_snowflake_probe`\n- `api/v1/suites.py` `trigger_suite_run` (manual run)\n- `services/orchestration_service.py` `_trigger_suites` (pipeline-success batch)\n- `worker/tasks.py` `_fire_schedule` (A7 scheduled run)\n\nThe failure *shape* was already unified in #302 (`mark_dispatch_failed`); this folds the surrounding `try: dispatch + commit / except: mark + commit + <policy>` into one helper.\n\nFixes #227.\n\n## How\n\nNew `run_dispatch.dispatch_or_fail(session, run) -> bool`:\n- dispatches, sets `celery_task_id`, commits → returns `True`\n- on broker failure: **logs the traceback**, `mark_dispatch_failed`, commits → returns `False`\n\nCallers own the policy for a `False` return:\n- HTTP paths (`probe`, `suites`) → `raise HTTPException(503)`\n- batch (`_trigger_suites`) → skip and carry on (one stuck broker can't drop the rest)\n- scheduled (`_fire_schedule`) → `return \"dispatch_failed\"`\n\nThe batch's create-all-then-dispatch-loop is preserved (the helper slots into the existing per-run loop).  import uuid
 from datetime import UTC, datetime
 
+from sqlalchemy.orm import Session

 from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
 from backend.app.db.models import Run
 from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app
@@ -55,6 +57,30 @@ def mark_dispatch_failed(run: Run) -> None:
     run.finished_at = datetime.now(UTC)
 
 
+def dispatch_or_fail(session: Session, run: Run) -> bool:
+    """Dispatch a committed queued ``run``; on broker failure record the canonical
+    terminal-failed shape. Returns ``True`` if dispatched, ``False`` if the broker
+    was unreachable (the run is now ``failed`` with ``finished_at`` set, committed).
+
+    The one copy of the dispatch + broker-failure block every trigger path shares
+    (probe, manual run, pipeline-success batch, scheduled run) so a never-dispatched
+    run is recorded — and its traceback logged — identically everywhere (#227). The
+    caller owns the *policy* for a ``False`` return: the HTTP paths surface 503; the
+    batch / scheduled paths skip the run and carry on. Mirrors ``run_suite``'s own
+    no-2-phase-commit contract (see ``dispatch_run``): the publish and the
+    ``celery_task_id`` commit aren't atomic, which is benign and self-correcting.
+    """
+    try:
+        run.celery_task_id = dispatch_run(run.id)
+        session.commit()
+        return True
+    except Exception:
+        log.exception("run_dispatch_failed", run_id=str(run.id))
+        mark_dispatch_failed(run)
+        session.commit()
+        return False
+
+
 def revoke_run(task_id: str | None) -> None:
     """Best-effort revoke of a dispatched run's Celery task.

```
I have enough to assess all three angles. Let me verify one final concern about the `from exc` removal — whether the original 503 HTTPException chained the broker traceback into anything observable (logging). In the OLD probe/suites, there was NO `log.exception` — the broker error was only carried via `from exc` on the HTTPException. FastAPI's default 503 handler does not log the chained cause's traceback. So the broker traceback was effectively lost in the OLD probe/suites paths. The NEW helper adds `log.exception("run_dispatch_failed")` — meaning probe/suites now LOG the broker traceback (an improvement, per angle B). Confirmed.

Now let me assess whether losing `from exc` chaining is meaningful. Since the new helper logs the full traceback via `log.exception`, and the HTTPException is raised separately, the traceback is captured in logs rather than chained. Net positive observability for probe/suites. The only real regression candidate is the lost `schedule_id` in the scheduled-path log.

I have all the information needed. Here are my findings.

```json
[
  {
    "file": "backend/app/services/run_dispatch.py",
    "line": 78,
    "summary": "Scheduled-path failure log loses schedule_id. The old _fire_schedule logged log.exception('schedule_dispatch_failed', schedule_id=..., run_id=...). The shared helper now logs 'run_dispatch_failed' with only run_id, and _fire_schedule no longer logs the schedule id anywhere on the failure branch (it just returns 'dispatch_failed').",
    "failure_scenario": "Broker is down when a cron schedule fires. The run is marked failed and a 'run_dispatch_failed' log line is emitted with run_id only. An operator triaging which SCHEDULE keeps failing to dispatch can no longer correlate the failure to a schedule from the log; they must join run_id -> Run.triggered_by ('schedule:<id>') out of band. Minor but real observability regression vs main. (Same applies, less severely, to the orchestration batch path which lost 'suite_dispatch_failed'.)"
  },
  {
    "file": "backend/app/services/run_dispatch.py",
    "line": 80,
    "summary": "The failure-path session.commit() (committing mark_dispatch_failed) is inside the except but unguarded; if THAT commit raises, dispatch_or_fail propagates a raw exception instead of returning False. This matches main's behavior, so it is NOT a regression — flagged only for completeness.",
    "failure_scenario": "Broker publish fails, then the DB connection also drops so the mark_dispatch_failed commit raises. dispatch_or_fail raises instead of returning False. In probe/suites the client gets a 500 (raw error) rather than the intended 503; in _fire_schedule/_trigger_suites the exception escapes the loop. Behavior is identical to the pre-refactor inline code, so no behavioral change was introduced — included as a known edge, not a new bug."
  }
]
```

Summary of the review across all three angles:

**Angle A (line-by-line):** No behavioral regressions found.
- `except Exception` in the helper matches all four originals (probe/suites used `except Exception as exc`, orchestration/`_fire_schedule` used bare `except Exception`) — same catch breadth.
- Removing `from exc` only drops exception chaining on the HTTPException; the broker traceback is now captured by the helper's `log.exception` instead, so nothing observable is lost (net gain — see Angle B).
- Success-path `session.commit()` is inside the try in both old and new code, so a post-publish commit failure marks the run failed in both — not a regression.
- `_fire_schedule` return contract (`'dispatch_failed'` / `'dispatched'`) is preserved exactly.
- `_trigger_suites` loop-continue-on-failure is preserved: the helper swallows the failure and returns False (ignored), so the loop proceeds and all created runs remain in `created`.

**Angle B (removed behavior):** Two log-event changes. Probe and suites previously did NOT log the broker error at all (only `from exc` chaining, which FastAPI's 503 handler doesn't surface) — they now log via `log.exception`, an improvement. The scheduled and batch paths LOST their structured `schedule_id`/`suite_dispatch_failed` context (finding #1) — the one genuine, if minor, observability regression.

**Angle C (cross-file tracer):** All test wiring still works. Every boom test and the autouse `stub_run_dispatch` fixture monkeypatch `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` on the canonical module object, and `dispatch_or_fail` calls `dispatch_run(...)` via module-global lookup at call time — so the patches are honored through the helper. `mark_dispatch_failed` is still called directly only inside the helper (and tested directly via `test_mark_dispatch_failed_sets_canonical_shape`); all four production call sites now route through `dispatch_or_fail`. No test asserts on the old log event names, so the rename breaks nothing.

The only actionable item is finding #1 (lost `schedule_id` in the scheduled-path failure log). Finding #2 is pre-existing and non-regressive.

# ── mutmut (mutation spikes) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Manual/periodic, NOT a CI gate (CONTRIBUTING rule 4a). mutmut 3.x copies the
# tree into ./mutants/, chdir's in, and runs pytest there — so `also_copy` must
# bring the whole importable package + tests + this config, and `-o addopts=`
# strips the repo's --cov flags (mutmut runs its own coverage plugin; a second
# --cov is a pytest usage error in-process). Per-spike: point `source_paths` at
# the one module you're spiking and narrow the test selection to its tests.
[tool.mutmut]
source_paths = ["backend/app/services/custom_sql.py"]
also_copy = ["backend", "pyproject.toml"]
pytest_add_cli_args_test_selection = ["backend/tests/services/test_custom_sql.py"]
pytest_add_cli_args = ["-o", "addopts="]
---precommit bandit---

> AGENT

Verified both. My honest assessment: **neither is serious, and I'm not filing issues for them** — here's the evidence.

## Bandit — the 4 findings (4× B608 Medium, + B101 asserts)

All four `B608` (hardcoded-SQL) findings live in **`backend/tests/`**:
- [test_custom_sql_gx.py:43](backend/tests/datasources/test_custom_sql_gx.py#L43) — `INSERT INTO {table}` with a test-controlled constant table name
- [test_custom_sql.py:161](backend/tests/services/test_custom_sql.py#L161) and [:246](backend/tests/services/test_custom_sql.py#L246) (×2) — `validate_query(f"…{keyword}…")` / `"…" + "\t"*50_000`, which **feed injection-/ReDoS-shaped strings into the SQL validator to prove it rejects them**. That's the security control being exercised, not a vulnerability.

They're not gated and not defects:
- CI runs `bandit -c pyproject.toml -r backend/app/`, and `pyproject.toml` has `exclude_dirs = ["backend/tests", "backend/alembic"]` + `skips = ["B101"]`. Tests are double-excluded; asserts are intentionally skipped (pytest idiom).
- **Bandit on the CI scope (`backend/app/`) at medium+ = 0 Medium, 0 High.** Production code is clean.

## mypy `celery_app` errors

These are 4× `[attr-defined]` "does not explicitly export attribute" on [test_run_dispatch.py](backend/tests/services/test_run_dispatch.py) — and they appear **only when mypy is pointed at a test file**. CI runs `mypy backend/app/` → **"Success: no issues found in 65 source files."** The cause is mypy's strict re-export rule: the test monkeypatches `run_dispatch.celery_app` (the standard monkeypatch idiom) rather than importing from origin. It's a test-style artifact in code mypy never checks in CI — no typing bug in shipped code.

## Conclusion

| Finding | Location | CI-gated? | Shipped-code defect? | Serious? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4× B608 Medium | `backend/tests/` | No (excluded) | No (false positives) | No |
| B101 asserts | `backend/tests/` | No (skipped) | No (pytest idiom) | No |
| mypy `celery_app` | `backend/tests/` | No (`mypy backend/app/`) | No (monkeypatch idiom) | No |

Per the working agreement, "Defects → GitHub issue" — but none of these are defects; they're test-only false positives in code outside the lint/type gates. Filing them would be noise in the week ledger, so I'm **not** filing. No similar pre-existing issues exist on the gated surface either: `backend/app/` is clean on both Bandit (medium+) and mypy.

If you'd ever want tests *inside* the lint/type gates (so these get a proper `# nosec`/`# type: ignore` instead of being out-of-scope), that's a deliberate scope-expansion decision — I can file *that* as an enhancement, but it's not a bug.

> AGENT

The alert is real but it's **not a security issue** — it's a CodeQL **quality "note"** (`py/unnecessary-lambda`, `security_severity_level: null`, severity `note`), just surfaced in the Security tab. And it's the **only** open code-scanning alert in the repo, so there are no similar pre-existing ones to file.

The finding is correct, though: [test_polling.py:184](backend/tests/worker/test_polling.py#L184) is `lambda: _FakeStore()` — a zero-arg wrapper around the zero-arg-constructor class `_FakeStore`, so `_FakeStore` can be passed directly. (Contrast the `lambda _t: provider` lambdas elsewhere — those take an arg and are *not* reducible.)

Since it's a one-line, behavior-identical fix that clears the only open alert, filing an issue would be more ledger-noise than the fix itself. I'll just clear it in a small chore PR (visible, not a silent fix). Let me do that.

Clears the alert.\n\n## Test\n\n\\`pytest backend/tests/worker/test_polling.py::test_recover_orchestration_gaps_task_uses_gap_lookback\\` → passes.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",

Done. Here's the full picture.

## Is it serious? No.

The alert is CodeQL **`py/unnecessary-lambda`** (alert #17) — severity **`note`**, `security_severity_level: null`. It's a **code-quality recommendation** that happens to render in the Security tab, **not a security vulnerability**. The finding itself is correct: [test_polling.py:184](backend/tests/worker/test_polling.py#L184) wrapped the zero-arg class `_FakeStore` in a zero-arg `lambda`, which is redundant.

## Similar / pre-existing alerts? None.

I queried all open code-scanning alerts — this was the **only one** in the repo. Nothing else to file.

## What I did

Rather than file an issue for a one-line, behavior-identical test fix (that'd be more ledger-noise than the change), I cleared it directly in **PR #321**:
- `lambda: _FakeStore()` → `_FakeStore` — `get_secret_store()` returns a fresh `_FakeStore()` either way.
- Left the `lambda _t: provider` lambdas elsewhere untouched — those take an argument and aren't reducible.
- Affected test passes locally; no app-code change.

CI is running on #321. It's a trivial test-only one-liner so a full multi-agent `/code-review` is overkill, but say the word and I'll run it (or merge once CI's green) — your call.
